This file's own `createThrowawayDatabase`/`withDatabaseName` are separate copies of the same logic (duplicated per package, matching this codebase's established precedent — see PR #46's review of the analogous `requireOrSkipDSN` duplication). 1	// Command api-service is the client-facing binary (Q1 DAO + Q2 REST API,
2	// bearer-token auth). Per decisions/go-layout-debate.md, this file is a
3	// thin wrapper — route registration and config loading live in
4	// internal/app and internal/config, not here.
5	package main
6

8		"database/sql"
9		"fmt"
10		"log"

15		"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
16		"loginid-takehome/internal/dao"

18		// internal/dao/postgres's own blank import of pgx/v5/stdlib already
19		// registers the "pgx" database/sql driver this file uses directly
20		// for the issuer database below — Go dedupes an import by package
21		// path, so this isn't a second registration, and no separate blank
22		// import of that package is needed here.
23		_ "loginid-takehome/internal/dao/postgres" // registers "postgres" and "cockroachdb"
24		_ "loginid-takehome/internal/dao/sqlite"   // registers "sqlite"

27	// sqlDriverNameFor maps cfg.DBDriver ("postgres"/"cockroachdb"/"sqlite" —
28	// dao.Register's driver names, per dao/factory.go) to the underlying
29	// database/sql driver name each backend package registers itself under
30	// (internal/dao/postgres/postgres.go: "pgx"; internal/dao/sqlite/sqlite.go:
31	// "sqlite") — needed only for LT-52's separate /healthz ping handle, since
32	// dao.Repository itself exposes no Ping and this is a thin sql.Open, not a
33	// second copy of either backend's connection logic.
34	func sqlDriverNameFor(daoDriver string) string {
35		switch daoDriver {
36		case "postgres", "cockroachdb":
37			return "pgx"
38		case "sqlite":
39			return "sqlite"
40		default:
41			return ""
42		}
43	}
44	
45	// validateAuthConfig catches exactly the live-review finding that
46	// motivated it: AUTH_JWT_ISSUER/AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE were never set in the
47	// deployed manifests, so the issuer minted iss-less tokens and the
48	// verifier compared aud against "" — every token "verified" against an
49	// empty audience check, which is a silent authorization hole (any
50	// signed token, for any audience, would have passed), not a visible
51	// failure. A verifier that quietly 401s everything, or an issuer that
52	// mints structurally-incomplete tokens, is worse than a crash-loop that
53	// names the exact missing variable — this is api-service's own
54	// mode-aware check, kept out of internal/config's shared Load() because
55	// AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE is meaningless to cmd/idp-connector (which has its
56	// own ConnectorJWTAudience instead) and a blanket check in the shared
57	// loader would wrongly fail that binary's startup too.
58	//
59	// AUTH_JWT_ISSUER is required in BOTH modes: the verifier checks it on
60	// every inbound token, and the issuer stamps it into every token it
61	// mints. AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE is required only in verifier mode — the
62	// issuer never reads its own audience config; it mints each token's
63	// `aud` from the per-client oauth_client.audience column instead
64	// (tokenhandler.go), so requiring it here would reject a valid issuer
65	// configuration that simply has no use for the variable.
66	func validateAuthConfig(cfg config.Config, mode app.Mode) error {
67		if cfg.AuthJWTIssuer == "" {
68			return fmt.Errorf("AUTH_JWT_ISSUER must be set in %s mode", mode)
69		}
70		if mode == app.ModeVerifier && cfg.AuthJWTAudience == "" {
71			return fmt.Errorf("AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE must be set in verifier mode")
72		}
73		return nil
74	}
75	
76	func main() {
77		cfg, err := config.Load()
78		if err != nil {
79			log.Fatalf("api-service: loading config: %v", err)
80		}
81		if err := validateAuthConfig(cfg, app.Mode(cfg.AppMode)); err != nil {
82			log.Fatalf("api-service: %v", err)
83		}
84	
85		addr := cfg.HTTPAddr
86		if addr == "" {
87			addr = ":8080"
88		}
89	
90		log.Printf("api-service: mode=%s listening on %s", cfg.AppMode, addr)
91	
92		var handler http.Handler
93		switch app.Mode(cfg.AppMode) {
94		case app.ModeVerifier:
95			// The verifying Deployment is the one that talks to the
96			// database (LT-39's search/retrieve handlers) — the issuer
97			// Deployment (below) never needs a Repository at all, since it
98			// only mints tokens.
99			//
100			// A failure here does NOT Fatal: on a cluster where the database
101			// doesn't exist yet (no DB_DRIVER/DB_DSN configured — e.g. before
102			// PR #29's Postgres StatefulSet lands), or DB_DRIVER is simply
103			// misconfigured, this process must still come up and serve
104			// /healthz rather than crash-loop the whole verifying Deployment
105			// over a dependency only two of its routes need. repo stays nil;
106			// NewVerifierRouter fails those two routes closed with 503
107			// instead of panicking on a nil Repository.
108			repo, err := dao.New(cfg.DBDriver, cfg.DBDSN)
109			if err != nil {
110				log.Printf("api-service: opening DAO repository: %v — /profiles/search and /profiles/{id} will return 503 until this is resolved", err)
111				repo = nil
112			}
113	
114			// A second, independent *sql.DB purely for /healthz's bounded
115			// connectivity probe (LT-52 criterion 5) — sql.Open never dials,
116			// it only validates the driver/DSN, so this never blocks
117			// startup; PingContext at request time is what actually checks
118			// connectivity, bounded to dbPingTimeout. Deliberately separate
119			// from repo above rather than routed through dao.Repository,
120			// which 05's contract doesn't expose a Ping through.
121			var pingDB *sql.DB
122			if driverName := sqlDriverNameFor(cfg.DBDriver); driverName != "" {
123				pingDB, err = sql.Open(driverName, cfg.DBDSN)
124				if err != nil {
125					log.Printf("api-service: opening /healthz DB ping handle: %v — /healthz will report db=down", err)
126					pingDB = nil
127				}
128			}
129	
130			handler = app.NewVerifierRouter(cfg, repo, pingDB)
131		case app.ModeIssuer:
132			// config.Load already fails startup if JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE is
133			// missing/unreadable in issuer mode (resolveSigningKeyPath) —
134			// LoadSigningKey failing here means the file is readable but not
135			// a valid RSA private key, a genuine misconfiguration this
136			// process cannot usefully run past (there is no "serve /healthz,
137			// fail closed" story for a router with no key at all: both of
138			// its routes need one). Fatal, not degrade — unlike the DAO
139			// repository above, which has a real story for "not there yet."
140			key, err := api.LoadSigningKey(cfg.JWTSigningKeyPath)
141			if err != nil {
142				log.Fatalf("api-service: loading JWT signing key: %v", err)
143			}
144	
145			// issuerDB, like the verifier's pingDB, is opened independently
146			// of whether it can be reached yet — sql.Open never dials. Empty
147			// IssuerDBDSN (not configured yet) leaves issuerDB nil;
148			// NewIssuerRouter's /auth/token then fails every grant closed
149			// with server_error rather than panicking on a nil ClientStore.
150			var issuerDB *sql.DB
151			if cfg.IssuerDBDSN != "" {
152				issuerDB, err = sql.Open("pgx", cfg.IssuerDBDSN)
153				if err != nil {
154					log.Printf("api-service: opening issuer database: %v — /auth/token will return server_error until this is resolved", err)
155					issuerDB = nil
156				}
157			} else {
158				log.Printf("api-service: ISSUER_DB_DSN/ISSUER_DB_DSN_FILE not set — /auth/token will return server_error until this is resolved")
159			}
160	
161			handler = app.NewIssuerRouter(cfg, key, issuerDB)
162		default:
163			// The unrecognized-mode fallback (NewRouter itself already logs
164			// loudly and treats it as verifier — see app.NewRouter's own doc
165			// comment) doesn't need the full verifier stack; NewRouter's
166			// /healthz alone covers it, matching the safer of the two route
167			// sets.
168			handler = app.NewRouter("api-service", app.Mode(cfg.AppMode))
169		}
170	
171		if err := http.ListenAndServe(addr, handler); err != nil {
172			log.Fatalf("api-service: %v", err)
173		}
174	}

# api-service's Deployment/Service, split out from deploy/manifests.yaml
# (LT-52) so the deploy workflow can apply it AFTER Postgres exists and
# the migration Job has completed — api-service is the one thing in this
# namespace whose startup depends on the schema already being there.
#
# replicas: 1, not 2 — per Naomi's (01) live review: RateLimiter,
# TouchCounter, and GrantLimiter are all in-process, per-pod state. She
# reproduced a 12-request burst passing a 10 req/min limit because the
# load balancer split it across two pods, each with its own independent
# counter — the same class of issue as api-service-issuer's replica
# count (Helena's review). Production path for real HA is a shared
# store (Redis), already named in the relevant code comments; not built
# for this take-home, so replicas stay at 1 rather than shipping a
# rate limiter that silently doesn't limit at the stated rate.
#
# strategy: Recreate, not the RollingUpdate default — per Tobias's (01)
# follow-up via Naomi/the PM: RollingUpdate's default maxSurge (25%,
# rounds up to 1 on a single-replica Deployment) briefly runs the old
# and new pod together during every future deploy, reopening the exact
# cross-replica limiter gap the replica-count drop above was meant to
# close, once per rollout. Recreate guarantees at most one pod exists
# at any time, at the cost of a few seconds of 503s at the Ingress
# while the old pod terminates and the new one becomes ready — accepted
# for a POC where the in-process limiters are security controls that
# assume exactly one pod, same trade already accepted for replicas: 1.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: api-service
  namespace: loginid-takehome
spec:
  replicas: 1
  strategy: {type: Recreate}
  selector: {matchLabels: {app: api-service}}
  template:
    metadata: {labels: {app: api-service}}
    spec:
      serviceAccountName: api-service
      securityContext: {runAsNonRoot: true, runAsUser: 65532, runAsGroup: 65532}
      containers:
        - name: api-service
          image: ghcr.io/wtvamp/loginid-takehome/api-service:IMAGE_TAG
          securityContext: {readOnlyRootFilesystem: true}
          resources:
            requests: {cpu: "100m", memory: "128Mi"}
            limits: {cpu: "500m", memory: "256Mi"}
          env:
            - {name: HTTP_ADDR, value: ":8080"}
            - {name: APP_MODE, value: verifier}
            - {name: DB_DRIVER, value: postgres}
            - {name: DB_DSN_FILE, value: /secrets/db/dsn}
            # Explicit :443 — the api-service-issuer Service only exposes
            # port 443 (-> targetPort 8080, plain HTTP; no in-cluster TLS,
            # only the public Ingress terminates TLS). No scheme/port
            # default matches that: bare http:// without a port defaults
            # to 80, which this Service never exposed — found live, a
            # real token minted with correct iss/aud still 401'd because
            # the JWKS fetch was refused and the verifier failed closed.
            - {name: AUTH_JWKS_URL, value: "http://api-service-issuer.loginid-takehome.svc.cluster.local:443/.well-known/jwks.json"}
            # Literal per PLANNING.md's Service boundaries config surface
            # (handoff-03-auth.md) — not secrets. Missing on both api-service
            # and api-service-issuer was why a minted token verified as
            # having no iss and an empty audience, failing jwtmiddleware's
            # explicit audience check (found live: a real token minted, but
            # every protected route still 401'd).
            - {name: AUTH_JWT_ISSUER, value: "https://auth.loginid-takehome.internal"}
            - {name: AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE, value: "loginid-api-service"}
            # StopgapAuthorizer's hard-coded (sub -> allowed record id)
            # seed: sub is the "qa-review-read-own" QA client's client_id
            # (the issuer mints Subject: clientID — LT-51), matched to
            # one of deploy/demo-data/review-profiles.sql's fixed demo
            # profile ids. Placeholder until real per-token authz lands.
            - {name: AUTHZ_QA_SUB, value: qa-review-read-own}
            - {name: AUTHZ_QA_ALLOWED_PROFILE_ID, value: "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001"}
          volumeMounts:
            - {name: db-runtime-credential, mountPath: /secrets/db, readOnly: true}
          readinessProbe: {httpGet: {path: /healthz, port: 8080}, initialDelaySeconds: 5}
          livenessProbe: {httpGet: {path: /healthz, port: 8080}, initialDelaySeconds: 10}
      volumes:
        - name: db-runtime-credential
          secret: {secretName: db-runtime-credential}
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata: {name: api-service, namespace: loginid-takehome}
spec:
  selector: {app: api-service}
  ports: [{port: 443, targetPort: 8080}]

		return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is a directory, not a file", path)
	}
	f, err := os.Open(path)
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not readable: %w", path, err)
	}
	defer func() { _ = f.Close() }()
	// Read one byte rather than open-close: an empty file (a Secret
	// mounted but never populated) opens without error and would
	// otherwise pass silently.
	var buf [1]byte
	if _, err := f.Read(buf[:]); err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is empty or unreadable: %w", path, err)
	}
	return path, nil
}

// resolveDSNFrom implements the DB_DSN_FILE-takes-precedence-over-DB_DSN
// pattern generically, parameterized by env var name so it serves both
// the main DAO's DSN and IssuerDBDSN without duplicating the logic.
func resolveDSNFrom(fileEnv, plainEnv string) (string, error) {
	if path := os.Getenv(fileEnv); path != "" {
		b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
		if err != nil {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: reading %s %q: %w", fileEnv, path, err)
		}
		return strings.TrimSpace(string(b)), nil
	}
	return os.Getenv(plainEnv), nil
}

And neither\n// check, however complete, can catch a manifest that's simply missing\n// the variable outright before a real request exercises the gap — only\n// a test that reads the manifest text itself can do that ahead of time,\n// which is what this declaration exists to make possible.\n//\n// service is \"api-service\" or \"idp-connector\" — the binary, not the\n// Deployment name (api-service and api-service-issuer are the SAME\n// binary in different modes). mode is \"verifier\"/\"issuer\" for\n// api-service (matching AppMode's own two values), or \"\" for\n// idp-connector, which has no mode concept.\nfunc RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode string) []string {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase service == \"api-service\" && mode == \"verifier\":\n\t\treturn []string{\"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER\", \"AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE\", \"AUTH_JWKS_URL\"}\n\tcase service == \"api-service\" && mode == \"issuer\":\n\t\t// AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE deliberately absent here — the issuer mints\n\t\t// each token's aud from the per-client oauth_client.audience\n\t\t// column (tokenhandler.go), never from its own config, so\n\t\t// requiring it would reject a valid issuer configuration for a\n\t\t// variable it has no use for (validateAuthConfig's own doc\n\t\t// comment states this same reasoning).\n\t\treturn []string{\"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER\"}\n\tcase service == \"idp-connector\":\n\t\treturn []string{\"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER\", \"AUTH_JWKS_URL\", \"CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE\"}\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n}"
}

11	// TestValidateAuthConfig_VerifierMode is the live-review finding's own
12	// enforcement site: AUTH_JWT_ISSUER and AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE both missing
13	// from the deployed manifests let the verifier silently compare every
14	// token's aud claim against "" — this must fail startup loudly instead,
15	// naming exactly which variable is missing.
16	func TestValidateAuthConfig_VerifierMode(t *testing.T) {
17		cases := []struct {
18			name          string
19			cfg           config.Config
20			wantErr       bool
21			wantErrSubstr string
22		}{
23			{
24				name:    "both set: ok",
25				cfg:     config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: "https://issuer.example", AuthJWTAudience: "loginid-api-service"},
26				wantErr: false,
27			},
28			{
29				name:          "issuer missing",
30				cfg:           config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: "", AuthJWTAudience: "loginid-api-service"},
31				wantErr:       true,
32				wantErrSubstr: "AUTH_JWT_ISSUER",
33			},
34			{
35				name:          "audience missing",
36				cfg:           config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: "https://issuer.example", AuthJWTAudience: ""},
37				wantErr:       true,
38				wantErrSubstr: "AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE",
39			},
40			{
41				name:    "both missing",
42				cfg:     config.Config{},
43				wantErr: true,
44				// Either name is acceptable — the point is a loud, named
45				// failure, not a specific ordering between the two checks.
46				wantErrSubstr: "AUTH_JWT_ISSUER",
47			},
48		}
49		for _, c := range cases {
50			t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
51				err := validateAuthConfig(c.cfg, app.ModeVerifier)
52				if c.wantErr && err == nil {
53					t.Fatalf("validateAuthConfig() = nil, want an error")
54				}
55				if !c.wantErr && err != nil {
56					t.Fatalf("validateAuthConfig() = %v, want nil", err)
57				}
58				if c.wantErr && !strings.Contains(err.Error(), c.wantErrSubstr) {
59					t.Errorf("error = %q, want it to name %q", err, c.wantErrSubstr)
60				}
61			})
62		}
63	}
64	
65	// TestValidateAuthConfig_IssuerMode confirms issuer mode only requires
66	// AUTH_JWT_ISSUER — AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE is meaningless here (the issuer
67	// mints each token's aud from the per-client oauth_client.audience
68	// column, not from global config), so an issuer Deployment with no
69	// AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE set at all must NOT be rejected.
70	func TestValidateAuthConfig_IssuerMode(t *testing.T) {
71		cases := []struct {
72			name    string
73			cfg     config.Config
74			wantErr bool
75		}{
76			{
77				name:    "issuer set, audience empty: ok",
78				cfg:     config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: "https://issuer.example", AuthJWTAudience: ""},
79				wantErr: false,
80			},
81			{
82				name:    "issuer missing",
83				cfg:     config.Config{AuthJWTIssuer: ""},
84				wantErr: true,
85			},
86		}
87		for _, c := range cases {
88			t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
89				err := validateAuthConfig(c.cfg, app.ModeIssuer)
90				if c.wantErr && err == nil {
91					t.Fatalf("validateAuthConfig() = nil, want an error")
92				}
93				if !c.wantErr && err != nil {
94					t.Fatalf("validateAuthConfig() = %v, want nil", err)
95				}
96				if c.wantErr && !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "AUTH_JWT_ISSUER") {
97					t.Errorf("error = %q, want it to name AUTH_JWT_ISSUER", err)
98				}
99			})
100		}
101	}

result
1	// Command idp-connector is the outbound-only binary (Q3: /auth, /identity
2	// against third-party IDPs). Per decisions/go-layout-debate.md, this file
3	// is a thin wrapper — route registration and config loading live in
4	// internal/app and internal/config, not here.
5	package main
6	
7	import (
8		"log"
9		"net/http"
10	
11		"loginid-takehome/internal/app"
12		"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
13		"loginid-takehome/internal/connector"
14	)
15	
16	func main() {
17		cfg, err := config.Load()
18		if err != nil {
19			log.Fatalf("idp-connector: loading config: %v", err)
20		}
21	
22		addr := cfg.HTTPAddr
23		if addr == "" {
24			addr = ":8081"
25		}
26	
27		// No real ABC/XYZ vendor exists for this take-home (LT-41's own
28		// non-goal) — IDP_ABC_BASE_URL unset (the common case in this
29		// environment) wires the self-contained StubVendorClient instead,
30		// seeded with one demo identity so the connector's full /auth ->
31		// /identity flow is exercisable end to end without a real vendor. If
32		// IDP_ABC_BASE_URL IS set, a real (or real-shaped) vendor is assumed
33		// reachable over HTTPS and HTTPVendorClient is used instead — this
34		// process does not silently fall back to the stub if that vendor
35		// turns out to be unreachable at request time; NewHTTPVendorClient
36		// only fails loudly at startup, on a non-https URL.
37		var vendor connector.VendorClient
38		if cfg.IDPABCBaseURL != "" {
39			httpVendor, err := connector.NewHTTPVendorClient(cfg.IDPABCBaseURL, nil)
40			if err != nil {
41				log.Fatalf("idp-connector: configuring vendor client: %v", err)
42			}
43			vendor = httpVendor
44			log.Printf("idp-connector: using HTTP vendor client at %s", cfg.IDPABCBaseURL)
45		} else {
46			stub := connector.NewStubVendorClient()
47			stub.Seed("demo-user", "demo-password", connector.Identity{
48				Name:          "Jane Demo",
49				Phone:         "+15555550100",
50				StreetAddress: "1 Demo Way",
51				Locality:      "Demoville",
52				Region:        "CA",
53				PostalCode:    "94000",
54				Country:       "US",
55			})
56			vendor = stub
57			log.Printf("idp-connector: IDP_ABC_BASE_URL not set — using in-process stub vendor (LT-41 non-goal: no real vendor integration)")
58		}
59	
60		log.Printf("idp-connector: listening on %s", addr)
61		if err := http.ListenAndServe(addr, app.NewConnectorRouter(cfg, vendor)); err != nil {
62			log.Fatalf("idp-connector: %v", err)
63		}
64	}

ok && mode != \"\" {\n\t\treturn mode\n\t}\n\treturn \"verifier\"\n}\n\n// envNamesAndValues returns every env entry's name mapped to its literal\n// value (only useful for APP_MODE, which is always a literal in these\n// manifests, never a secretKeyRef) — and, separately, the full set of\n// NAMES present regardless of how each is sourced (literal value,\n// secretKeyRef, or a *_FILE path to a mounted Secret), since this test\n// only needs to confirm the variable NAME is set on the container, not\n// read any actual secret value.\nfunc envNamesAndValues(doc k8sManifestDoc, containerIdx int) (names map[string]bool, values map[string]string) {\n\tnames = make(map[string]bool)\n\tvalues = make(map[string]string)\n\tfor _, e := range doc.Spec.Template.Spec.Containers[containerIdx].Env {\n\t\tnames[e.Name] = true\n\t}\n\t// Re-decode is unnecessary — Env entries in these manifests are\n\t// always {name, value} (literal) in the specific case this helper\n\t// cares about (APP_MODE), so the typed struct above already captured\n\t// what's needed via a second, value-aware pass below.\n\treturn names, values\n}\n\n// TestManifestsSetRequiredAuthEnvVars is the systemic fix for two\n// separate live-review incidents in one day, both the same shape: a\n// Deployment's manifest silently omitted an auth env var its own\n// running mode required, and nothing caught it until a real request\n// exposed the gap in production. This test makes the requirement\n// itself — not just each binary's own startup check — enforced in CI:\n// it reads config.RequiredAuthEnvVars (the single source of truth\n// cmd/api-service and cmd/idp-connector's own startup validation also\n// reads) and asserts every real Deployment in deploy/api-service.yaml\n// and deploy/manifests.yaml actually sets everything its own mode\n// requires, by parsing the manifest text directly — not by trusting\n// that whoever last edited a Deployment remembered to keep it in sync.\nfunc TestManifestsSetRequiredAuthEnvVars(t *testing.T) {\n\troot := repoRoot(t)\n\tvar allDeployments []struct {\n\t\tfile string\n\t\tdoc  k8sManifestDoc\n\t}\n\tfor _, relPath := range []string{\n\t\tfilepath.Join(\"deploy\", \"api-service.yaml\"),\n\t\tfilepath.Join(\"deploy\", \"manifests.yaml\"),\n\t} {\n\t\tpath := filepath.Join(root, relPath)\n\t\tfor _, doc := range parseDeployments(t, path) {\n\t\t\tallDeployments = append(allDeployments, struct {\n\t\t\t\tfile string\n\t\t\t\tdoc  k8sManifestDoc\n\t\t\t}{relPath, doc})\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tchecked := 0\n\tfor _, entry := range allDeployments {\n\t\tdoc := entry.doc\n\t\tif len(doc.Spec.Template.Spec.Containers) == 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\t// This test only has an opinion about the container matching a\n\t\t// binary it recognizes — a Deployment could in principle run a\n\t\t// sidecar or an unrelated image in the same pod spec; check\n\t\t// every container, not just the first, so a required var set on\n\t\t// the wrong container in the pod spec is still caught rather\n\t\t// than silently passing because container[0] happened to be\n\t\t// something else.\n\t\tfor ci, c := range doc.Spec.Template.Spec.Containers {\n\t\t\tservice, recognized := serviceForImage(c.Image)\n\t\t\tif !recognized {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tenvNames := make(map[string]bool, len(c.Env))\n\t\t\tappMode := \"\"\n\t\t\tfor _, e := range c.Env {\n\t\t\t\tenvNames[e.Name] = true\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// A second pass to read APP_MODE's literal value — needs the\n\t\t\t// raw YAML node, not just the name, since k8sManifestDoc's Env\n\t\t\t// entries above only capture Name. Re-parse just this one\n\t\t\t// field via a value-carrying variant, scoped to this single\n\t\t\t// container, rather than complicating the shared struct with\n\t\t\t// a field only this one lookup needs.\n\t\t\tappMode = appModeValue(t, root, entry.file, doc.Metadata.Name, ci)\n\n\t\t\tmode := appMode\n\t\t\tif mode == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\tmode = \"verifier\"\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\trequired := config.RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode)\n\t\t\tif len(required) == 0 {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tchecked++\n\n\t\t\tvar missing []string\n\t\t\tfor _, name := range required {\n\t\t\t\tif 13	// migrationSQL reads a goose-format migration file and returns just the Up
14	// section's SQL — the real deliverable in migrations/{shared,postgres}/,
15	// not a duplicated copy of the schema. Applying the actual migration files
16	// in tests is what keeps this test harness honest: if the migrations
17	// drift from what the Go code expects, the integration tests fail, not
18	// just a hand-maintained fixture.
19	func migrationSQL(t *testing.T, path string) string {
20		t.Helper()
21		b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
22		if err != nil {
23			t.Fatalf("reading migration %s: %v", path, err)
24		}
25		content := string(b)
26		upStart := strings.Index(content, "-- +goose Up")
27		downStart := strings.Index(content, "-- +goose Down")
28		if upStart == -1 || downStart == -1 {
29			t.Fatalf("migration %s missing +goose Up/Down markers", path)
30		}
31		return content[upStart+len("-- +goose Up") : downStart]
32	}
33	
34	// splitSQLStatements splits a migration's Up section into individual
35	// statements on ";" — used only for migrations that must NOT run as one
36	// multi-statement transaction (see the 00002 phone-CHECK migration's own
37	// comment). "--" comment lines are stripped first: this file's own
38	// prose comments use ordinary English semicolons ("...split; see the
39	// cockroachdb migration's own comment."), which a naive split-on-";"
40	// would otherwise cut mid-sentence and misidentify as a statement
41	// boundary. None of the actual SQL statements' string literals contain a
42	// ";", so splitting the comment-free text is safe — this is not a
43	// general-purpose SQL parser.
44	func splitSQLStatements(sqlText string) []string {
45		var codeOnly strings.Builder
46		for _, line := range strings.Split(sqlText, "\n") {
47			if idx := strings.Index(line, "--"); idx != -1 {
48				line = line[:idx]
49			}
50			codeOnly.WriteString(line)
51			codeOnly.WriteString("\n")
52		}
53	
54		var out []string
55		for _, stmt := range strings.Split(codeOnly.String(), ";") {
56			if strings.TrimSpace(stmt) == "" {
57				continue
58			}
59			out = append(out, stmt)
60		}
61		return out
62	}

Key Technical Concepts: - Go modules, `database/sql`, `net/http`, goose migrations (`-- +goose Up/Down`, `-- +goose StatementBegin/StatementEnd` for multi-statement/dollar-quoted blocks, `-- +goose NO TRANSACTION`), `github.com/pressly/goose/v3` as a real Go-library migration runner (not just a CLI) for tests. - PHC (Password Hashing Competition) string format for Argon2id hashes (`«REDACTED-ARGON2»`) — self-describing, parsed and verified from its own embedded parameters, never assumed to match compile-time constants (the "trap" Priya named). - RFC 6749 OAuth2 client-credentials grant; timing-oracle closure via constant-cost Argon2id comparison against a fixed dummy hash on every non-match path. - Rate-limiting/backoff design: `api.GrantLimiter`/`InProcessGrantLimiter` (existing, reused not rebuilt), per-(caller, attempted-identity) rate limiting via HMAC-SHA256-hashed composite keys, bounded tracked-key cardinality with sweep-based eviction (mirroring `JWKSCache`'s unknown-kid cap pattern), and — critically — Marcus Ilori's ruling that **fail-open past a capacity cap is unacceptable** ("degrades to the exact original weakness") — the correct pattern is a **shared overflow bucket** (a second, separate `GrantLimiter` instance keyed by one fixed constant string) that still provides coarser-but-nonzero protection once the tracked-key table is full. - JWKS caching: `ErrJWKSStale` (cache exists, aged past max staleness) vs. the newly-added `ErrJWKSUnreachable` (no cache exists yet at all — cold start) — both must map to the same `AuditJWKSUnavailable` audit-event kind, distinct from `AuditAuthnFailure`. Failure-streak-based rate-limited logging (`log.Printf` once per failure-streak transition, not per request) with a coarse `classifyJWKSFetchError` bucketing (`request_build_error`/`network_error`/`http_status_error`/`response_read_error`/`response_parse_error`/`unknown`) and a `jwksURLHost` helper extracting just the host for log safety. - `os.Getenv("CI")` as GitHub Actions' reliable unconditional CI-detection signal (confirmed against `.github/workflows/pr-check.yml`'s `build-lint-test` job running on `ubuntu-latest`), used to convert `t.Skip` (silently "passing" without ever exercising a backend) into `t.Fatalf` naming the missing env var, when a required real-backend test's DSN is absent specifically in CI — while preserving `t.Skip` for local runs without a database. - Cross-package test isolation via per-package `TestMain(m *testing.M)` creating a throwaway Postgres/CockroachDB database (`CREATE DATABASE test_<pkg>_<random>`, migrate once, `DROP DATABASE` at exit) — closing a real race where two Go test packages both pointed at the same shared database and raced on `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` (non-atomic `IF NOT EXISTS` DDL race in Postgres). `WITH (FORCE)` (Postgres 13+-only) safe only in the Postgres-only package's cleanup; the conformance package's shared postgres/cockroachdb cleanup deliberately omits it (CockroachDB doesn't support it) and instead relies on `t.Cleanup`/`m.Run()` ordering guarantees that all per-test connections are already closed by the time `TestMain`'s own cleanup runs. - Single-source-of-truth config validation pattern: `config.RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode string) []string` declared once in `internal/config/config.go`, read by BOTH each binary's own startup `validateAuthConfig` (which checks raw `os.Getenv` directly, not the already-loaded `cfg` struct fields, so it operates on the identical primitive a manifest-parsing test also checks) AND a new systemic test (`internal/config/manifest_test.go`) that parses the real Kubernetes YAML manifests via `gopkg.in/yaml.v3` and cross-checks. - `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`'s `yaml.NewDecoder(io.Reader)` + repeated `.Decode()` calls to `io.EOF` (checked via `err.Error() == "EOF"` in this codebase's style) for parsing multi-document (`---`-separated) Kubernetes manifest files. - Kubernetes manifest structure: `deploy/api-service.yaml` (api-service verifier-mode Deployment, split out per LT-52 to sequence after the Postgres migration Job) and `deploy/manifests.yaml` (everything else, including `api-service-issuer` and `idp-connector` Deployments) — both are real files I read in full during this session. - Git worktree discipline: never `git checkout`/`switch` in the shared root tree; every feature/fix branch gets its own worktree under `/Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID-worktrees/<name>`, each with its own `git config --worktree user.name/email` set to the Renata Cole persona identity. - `gitleaks detect --log-opts="origin/main..HEAD"` run before every push, per an earlier lesson in this session (PR #39 initially failed CI's gitleaks scan on a comment that pattern-matched `generic-api-key`; the real fix required rewording AND squashing the branch to remove the flagged blob from history entirely, not just superseding it with a later commit). - `staticcheck` (installed via `go install honnef.co/go/tools/cmd/staticcheck@latest`, binary at `$(go env GOPATH)/bin/staticcheck`) run alongside `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` as a standard pre-push check established partway through this session after PR #40 failed CI lint (S1016 struct-literal-to-type-conversion findings, an unused const). - Attribution convention: every commit ends with `Story:`, `Work-By:`, `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`, `Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_011Cuba4JUvrsQCrTdUj4yzk` trailers; `internal/config/config.go` — added (appended after `resolveDSNFrom`): ```go // RequiredAuthEnvVars is the single source of truth for which auth-related // env vars a given (service, mode) combination MUST have set to function // correctly — read by both this process's own startup validation // (cmd/api-service/main.go's validateAuthConfig, cmd/idp-connector/main.go's // equivalent) and, independently, by internal/config's own // TestManifestsSetRequiredAuthEnvVars, which parses the actual deployed // manifests (deploy/api-service.yaml, deploy/manifests.yaml) and asserts // every Deployment sets everything its own (service, mode) requires. // // [... extensive doc comment about the two live incidents ...] // // service is "api-service" or "idp-connector" — the binary, not the // Deployment name (api-service and api-service-issuer are the SAME // binary in different modes). mode is "verifier"/"issuer" for // api-service (matching AppMode's own two values), or "" for // idp-connector, which has no mode concept. func RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode string) []string { switch { case service == "api-service" && mode == "verifier": return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER", "AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE", "AUTH_JWKS_URL"} case service == "api-service" && mode == "issuer": return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER"} case service == "idp-connector": return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER", "AUTH_JWKS_URL", "CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE"} default: return nil } } ``` - uses `package config` internal test package; both can coexist in one directory) — final (post-simplification) version: ```go package config_test import ( "os" "path/filepath" "runtime" "strings" "testing" "gopkg.in/yaml.v3" "loginid-takehome/internal/config" ) type k8sManifestDoc struct { Kind string `yaml:"kind"` Metadata struct { Name string `yaml:"name"` } `yaml:"metadata"` Spec struct { Template struct { Spec struct { Containers []struct { Image string `yaml:"image"` Env []struct { Name string `yaml:"name"` Value string `yaml:"value"` } `yaml:"env"` } `yaml:"containers"` } `yaml:"spec"` } `yaml:"template"` } `yaml:"spec"` } func parseDeployments(t *testing.T, path string) []k8sManifestDoc { t.Helper() f, err := os.Open(path) if err != nil { t.Fatalf("opening %s: %v", path, err) } defer func() { _ = f.Close() }() var deployments []k8sManifestDoc dec := yaml.NewDecoder(f) for { var doc k8sManifestDoc if err := dec.Decode(&doc); err != nil { if err.Error() == "EOF" { break } t.Fatalf("decoding a YAML document from %s: %v", path, err) } if doc.Kind == "Deployment" { deployments = append(deployments, doc) } } return deployments } func serviceForImage(image string) (service string, recognized bool) { switch { case strings.Contains(image, "/idp-connector:"): return "idp-connector", true case strings.Contains(image, "/api-service:"): return "api-service", true default: return "", false } } func TestManifestsSetRequiredAuthEnvVars(t *testing.T) { root := repoRoot(t) var allDeployments []struct { file string doc k8sManifestDoc } for _, relPath := range []string{ filepath.Join("deploy", "api-service.yaml"), filepath.Join("deploy", "manifests.yaml"), } { path := filepath.Join(root, relPath) for _, doc := range parseDeployments(t, path) { allDeployments = append(allDeployments, struct { file string doc k8sManifestDoc }{relPath, doc}) } } checked := 0 for _, entry := range allDeployments { doc := entry.doc for _, c := range doc.Spec.Template.Spec.Containers { service, recognized := serviceForImage(c.Image) if !recognized { continue } envNames := make(map[string]bool, len(c.Env)) appMode := "" for _, e := range c.Env { envNames[e.Name] = true if e.Name == "APP_MODE" { appMode = e.Value } } mode := appMode if mode == "" { mode = "verifier" } required := config.RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode) if len(required) == 0 { continue } checked++ var missing []string for _, name := range required { if !envNames[name] { missing = append(missing, name) } } if len(missing) > 0 { t.Errorf("%s: Deployment %q, container %q (service=%s, mode=%s) is missing required env var(s): %v", entry.file, doc.Metadata.Name, c.Image, service, mode, missing) } } } if checked == 0 { t.Fatalf("no recognized Deployment (api-service or idp-connector image) was found in deploy/api-service.yaml or deploy/manifests.yaml — this test's own matching logic may have drifted from the real manifests, silently checking nothing") } } func repoRoot(t *testing.T) string { t.Helper() _, thisFile, _, ok := runtime.Caller(0) if !ok { t.Fatal("could not determine test file location") } return filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(thisFile), "..", "..") } ``` **This file had NOT yet been built/tested/run at the time work was interrupted for the urgent rebase** — I had just finished writing the simplified version (after discarding an earlier, Errors and fixes: - **PR #39 gitleaks failure**: a comment fragment in `tokenhandler.go` pattern-matched `generic-api-key`. Fixed initially by rewording (insufficient — the flagged commit stayed in history), then properly fixed by rebasing onto latest main and squashing the whole branch to one commit via `git reset --soft origin/main` (after verifying `git merge-base HEAD origin/main` exactly equaled `origin/main`'s tip, to avoid accidentally reverting unrelated main-branch changes) — confirmed via `gitleaks detect --log-opts="origin/main..HEAD"` showing zero findings against the actual pushed remote ref. - **Nolan's real bug in LT-41's identity rate limiter** (PR #40): `recordFailure`/`recordSuccess` bypassed the `maxTrackedIdentities` cap check that `allow()` correctly enforced, letting the underlying `GrantLimiter`'s maps grow unbounded under a flood. Fixed via a single `trackOrCheck(key string, admitIfRoom bool) bool` choke point all three methods route through. - **Marcus's follow-up ruling** on the same file: fail-open past the cap was itself unacceptable ("degrades to the exact original weakness"). Fixed by adding a second `overflow *api.InProcessGrantLimiter` instance with more aggressive constants (`identityOverflowBaseBackoff = 5s`, `identityOverflowMaxBackoff = 10m`, `identityOverflowAlertThreshold = 3`) and a fixed `identityOverflowKey` constant — `allow`/`recordFailure`/`recordSuccess` now route to this shared bucket instead of failing open once `trackOrCheck` reports no room. - **Oren's separator-ambiguity note**: `hashIdentity`'s pre-joined `body.Phone + "|" + body.Name` risked collision if either field contained `|`. Fixed by making `hashIdentity` variadic (`func hashIdentity(callerSub string, identityParts ...string) string`) and passing phone/name as two separate arguments, hashed with individual null-byte separators, rather than pre-joining with a printable delimiter. - **Nolan's real bug in the goose migration** (surfaced via Theo's actual live deploy failure, not a review): `CREATE FUNCTION ... $$ ... $$` in `migrations/issuer/00001_initial_schema.sql` broke goose's real statement-splitter (SQLSTATE 42601). Fixed with `-- +goose StatementBegin`/`StatementEnd` markers — but my FIRST attempt at this fix accidentally broke the goose parser AGAIN by including the literal text `-- +goose StatementBegin` inside an explanatory prose comment one line above the real marker, which goose's parser also matched as an annotation attempt. Fixed by rewording the prose to avoid that exact string pattern. - **My own design mistake in `internal/dao/postgres/testmain_test.go`**: initially wrote `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS <name> WITH (FORCE)` in the `internal/dao/conformance` package's shared `createThrowawayDatabase` — but that function is shared by BOTH the postgres AND cockroachdb fixtures, and CockroachDB does not support the Postgres-13+-only `WITH (FORCE)` syntax (would fail with a syntax error, not silently ignore it). Caught this myself before running it, fixed by using plain `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS <name>` in the conformance package's shared helper (relying on `t.Cleanup`/`m.Run()` ordering guarantees instead), while keeping `WITH (FORCE)` in the `internal/dao/postgres` package's own copy (verified via grep that file is genuinely never used for CockroachDB). - **`t.Skipf` after `t.Fatalf` is dead code but harmless** — Oren noted the `return ""` after `t.Fatalf`/`t.Skipf` calls in `requireOrSkipDSN`-style helpers is unreachable but required by Go's compiler for a function with a return type; not a defect. - **Test-design bug I introduced and self-corrected**: in `TestIdentityRateLimiter_TypoIsolation`, I initially asserted an immediate retry after one failure should still be allowed — this was WRONG given the existing `identityLimiterBaseBackoff = 1s` design (backoff starts after even the first failure, matching `GrantLimiter`'s established behavior elsewhere in the codebase) — not a bug in the code, a wrong assumption in my own test. Fixed by removing that specific assertion and keeping only the cross-identity isolation check. - **PR #46's rebase conflicts** (the task in progress at summary time) — see "Current Work" below; not yet resolved. 5. 1	# LT-38 refinement — S5: conformance suite (cross-backend behavioral conformance)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S5 (LT-38).
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why pulled now
8	
9	LT-36 and LT-37's refinement (see `refinement/LT-36-LT-37.md`) cited this suite as the sole enforcement site for roughly half their own acceptance criteria and added a hard rule that neither story can be marked Done until LT-38 lands and those criteria actually pass. Pulling LT-38 now is what closes that gap — it's the next dependency in the chain (`Depends on: S3, S4`), and refining it now means it's ready to run the moment either backend PR lands, rather than sitting as a bottleneck after the fact.
10	
11	## Correction found before drafting (citation error in `backlog.md` itself, not introduced here)
12	
13	`backlog.md`'s S5 description cites "the secret-state rules (§5/§6.11)" for the `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship. Checked against `multi-db-strategy.md` directly: **§6.11 is item 11 in §6's numbered list — "`deletion_log` carries no PII" — a different guarantee entirely.** The `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship is item 12 (unlabeled with an explicit "§6.12" in the source doc's own prose, but numerically next in the same list, referred to there only as "item 12"). `is_active`'s citation as §6.10 is correct — that one checks out exactly. Fixed below by citing item 12 correctly and not repeating the backlog's §6.11 mislabel.
14	
15	## Acceptance outcome
16	
17	No public-URL surface (same as LT-35/36/37) — this is a test suite, not a deployable behavior change. "Done" means the suite exists once, runs against all three backends from one table-driven test, and every guarantee below is asserted as a specific, named test case (not a general "seems to work" pass) — modeled on the frozen-checksum-plus-structural-diff pattern Oren built for LT-35's conformance test, since 05's own §7 explicitly warns that signature conformance (LT-35) proves nothing about behavioral conformance (this story) — two implementations can match every declaration and still return different rows for the same call.
18	
19	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S5, corrected and expanded)
20	
21	- [ ] The suite is written once against the `Repository` interface and executed against all three backends (postgres, cockroachdb, sqlite) from one table-driven test file, not duplicated per package. Enforcement site: a single test function parameterized over backend, not three near-identical copies.
22	- [ ] Asserts identical `Search` result sets and ordering across backends for ASCII input — this suite is `multi-db-strategy.md`'s named enforcement site for this guarantee; there is no other. Named test case: a fixed dataset seeded identically on all three backends via each backend's own `Create` (not a caller-supplied ID, since neither backend exposes that path), a fixed query, asserting identical results and order. **Rows are compared by every `ProfileQuery`-relevant field except the DAO-generated `ID`**; the suite matches corresponding rows across backends by a fixed, test-controlled non-ID field (e.g., seeded `Name` or `Phone` values guaranteed unique in the fixture set) rather than assuming identical literal `ID` values, since ID generation is backend-specific (server-side on Postgres/CockroachDB, Go-side on SQLite, per LT-36/LT-37's own acceptance criteria) and a literal row-for-row comparison including `ID` would fail on every run regardless of whether `Search` itself behaves identically.
23	- [ ] Asserts `errors.Is` sentinel parity for every condition in the §4 translation table, across all three backends — named test case per condition (not one combined test), consistent with LT-36/LT-37's "not-Done-until-LT-38" dependency: this is the specific enforcement site those stories were waiting on.
24	- [ ] Asserts the `requires_secret`/`secret_state` relationship (§6, item 12 — corrected citation, see above) as a named test: creating a credential with `secret_state = 'set'` against a method whose `requires_secret = false` returns `ErrInvalidCredential`, on all three backends.
25	- [ ] Asserts `is_active` enforcement (§6.10) as a named test: creating a credential against a deactivated `auth_method` returns `ErrInvalidMethod`, on all three backends — per the doc's own note that deactivation must not invalidate *existing* credentials, so the test also asserts an existing credential against a since-deactivated method still reads back successfully.
26	- [ ] Asserts empty-string rejection on every `*string` filter field in `ProfileQuery` — one named test case per field (`Name`, `Phone`, `Region`, `Country`), not one combined test, per the "absent, never empty" convention.
27	- [ ] Retention/deletion assertions from `multi-db-strategy.md` §7's own minimum list, each as a named test case: a zero-value `RetentionClass` (`""`) returns `ErrInvalidArgument`; `maxRows` outside `1..10_000` returns `ErrInvalidArgument`; `OldestSurvivingAt` is non-nil when rows remain (including when nothing was old enough to delete) and nil only when the class is empty; `Drained` is false while deletable rows remain at the batch limit and true on the final batch; every delete writes exactly one `deletion_log` row in the same transaction with the reason code the method itself asserts; an already-cancelled context returns before deleting anything; `deletion_log` carries no PII-shaped column (§6.11 — correctly cited here, this is the actual §6.11 guarantee).
28	- [ ] The CockroachDB-specific retry path is exercised for real: at least one test forces a genuine SQLSTATE `40001` (e.g. two concurrent transactions racing on the same row) against a real CockroachDB instance/testcontainer and asserts the operation succeeds after retry, not a mocked retry.
29	- [ ] CI (04's pipeline) runs this suite against every backend including CockroachDB, not just Postgres+SQLite — flagged to 04 if their pipeline configuration doesn't do this; enforcement site is the CI job matrix/config itself, not this suite's own code.
30	- [ ] Following the model Oren built for LT-35: the suite's test data / expected-behavior assertions state, in a comment, which parts are derived directly from `multi-db-strategy.md`'s prose (the guarantees list in §7 and the numbered items in §6) and, where practical, carry the same frozen-copy-plus-checksum protection so a later revision to those sections forces a human re-check rather than the suite silently asserting a stale guarantee. Scoped down from LT-35's version since this suite asserts *behavior*, not *signatures* — a full checksum-gated transcription may not be practical for prose guarantees the way it was for Go signatures: LT-35's checksum worked because §1–§4 is one contiguous block, hashed once, while this suite's guarantees are scattered across §4, §6 (items 10/12), §7, and §3c — non-contiguous fragments that would each need a separately-pinned hash boundary, fragile to unrelated prose edits nearby. At minimum, each test case's doc comment names the specific `multi-db-strategy.md` line/item it enforces, so a future doc change is at least greppable.
31

55	## PO acceptance review — CI status, joint pass 2026-09-13
56	
57	**Reviewer:** Naomi Voss (PO). Checked `internal/dao/conformance/testutil_test.go` and the live `pr-check.yml` on `main` directly, not the "Build, lint, test" job's green status alone.
58	
59	**Finding: the CI acceptance criterion is not met, and "Build, lint, test" going green does not mean what it appears to mean.** `newPostgresFamilyFixture` (shared by the `postgres` and `cockroachdb` backends) explicitly skips (`t.Skipf`) when its DSN env var (`TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`/`TEST_COCKROACHDB_DSN`) isn't set — by its own comment, "this suite asserts behavior, it doesn't stand up infrastructure." `pr-check.yml` never sets either variable and never provisions a Postgres/CockroachDB service or testcontainer. Only the SQLite fixture is genuinely self-contained (in-memory). **Net effect: every Postgres and CockroachDB conformance test — including `TestConformance_CockroachDB_RealSerializationRetry`, this story's own flagship acceptance criterion — silently skips on every CI run, and Go reports a skip as neither pass nor fail, so nothing currently flags this as a problem.** `go test ./... -race` returning success proves SQLite's conformance tests passed; it proves nothing about the other two backends.
60	
61	**Ruling (team-lead):** the suite itself is well-built and correctly scoped — this is a pipeline-wiring gap, not a defect in the tests. Fix in flight: Theo adds real Postgres/CockroachDB service containers to `pr-check.yml`; Renata makes the fixtures fail loudly under `CI=true` rather than skip, so a future regression of this exact gap can't hide behind a green build again.
62	
63	**Verdict: LT-38 FAILS criterion "CI runs every backend including CockroachDB" — fix in flight, not accepted yet.** Every other behavioral criterion is scored as verified, from two independent sources: Renata's real-backend test runs (once the fixtures are wired for real) plus this joint review's own live checks against the actual deployed Postgres instance (masking, full reads, scope separation — recorded in `refinement/LT-36-LT-37.md` and `LT-39.md`). Not a blanket fail on the suite's substance — narrowly, honestly, a fail on the one criterion that was silently unmet.
64	
65	## Finding from Theo's real-CI run — cross-package database race, fixed
66	
67	**2026-09-13, Theo's #47 (real Postgres/CockroachDB service containers) run against the real GitHub Actions runner.** Good news first: `TestConformance_CockroachDB_RealSerializationRetry` — this story's own flagship criterion — passed against a real CockroachDB instance, and the whole conformance package passed for all three backends. But `internal/dao/postgres` failed 5 integration tests at the migration-apply step: `ERROR: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "pg_extension_name_index" (SQLSTATE 23505)`, and on other runs `ERROR: operator class "gin_trgm_ops" does not exist for access method "gin" (SQLSTATE 42704)` (run 34761945847).
68	
69	**Diagnosis:** `go test ./...` runs packages concurrently by default, and `internal/dao/conformance` and `internal/dao/postgres` both pointed at the SAME `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`-named database, each re-running `goose`-equivalent migration SQL per test. Two concurrent `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm` calls hit a known Postgres race — `IF NOT EXISTS` is not atomic across sessions — so the loser saw the unique-constraint violation, and (since its own create had lost) then saw the `gin_trgm_ops` operator class missing when the migration went on to build an index using it. Theo's local run passed because it happened not to hit the interleaving; the bug was always there, just timing-dependent. Exactly this story's own §7 class of problem, one layer up: the fixture assumed sole ownership of the target database, and nothing enforced that assumption across packages — each package's existing per-TEST schema isolation (`CREATE SCHEMA`) only ever prevented same-package collisions, since `pg_extension` is database-wide, not schema-scoped.
70	
71	**Ruled fix (PM): a per-package throwaway database, not `go test -p 1`.** A flag that happens to serialize package execution is a hope about test-runner internals, not a structural control, and it would silently mask this exact bug for anyone running the suite locally with the default parallelism.
72	
73	**Fixed:** both `internal/dao/postgres` and `internal/dao/conformance` gained a `TestMain` that creates one throwaway database for that whole test binary's run (`CREATE DATABASE test_<pkg>_<random>` on the target server, connect to it instead, apply migrations once, `DROP DATABASE` at exit) — `internal/dao/conformance`'s `TestMain` does this independently for both Postgres and CockroachDB, since a local run might only have one of the two DSNs configured. Existing per-test schema isolation is unchanged and still runs inside each package's own throwaway database. Verified by reproducing the exact race locally against a real Postgres container (running both packages concurrently reliably failed with the identical errors before this fix, and passed consistently across repeated runs after it) and confirming no leftover throwaway databases after a full run.
74	||||||| parent of 1615e9b (fix: fail (not skip) real-backend tests when CI is set and DSN is absent)
75	=======
76	## Finding from the joint review — CI silently skipped every Postgres/CockroachDB run, fixed
77	
78	**2026-09-13, Naomi's live review.** `internal/dao/conformance`'s `newPostgresFamilyFixture` (and the equivalent fixtures in `internal/dao/postgres/testutil_test.go` and `internal/api/clientstore_pg_test.go`) `t.Skip`s when `TEST_POSTGRES_DSN`/`TEST_COCKROACHDB_DSN` are unset — and `pr-check` never set them. Every Postgres and CockroachDB conformance/integration test, including `TestConformance_CockroachDB_RealSerializationRetry` (this story's own named enforcement site for the retry-path criterion above), has been silently skipped in CI all along. A green `pr-check` run meant "SQLite conformance proven," never "all three backends proven" — exactly the gap criterion 4 in this doc's own PO review script asks to be confirmed, and it was never actually checked because nobody looked past the green checkmark to see three backends' worth of tests reporting `SKIP`, not `PASS`.
79	
80	**Root cause is 05's own §7 principle, applied to CI rather than to a database assertion:** a skipped assertion that reads as a pass in the CI summary is worse than a red build. `t.Skip` and `t.Fail` are visually similar in a passing/failing sense at the job level (both leave the overall `go test` exit code green when every non-skipped test also passes) but carry opposite meanings for what was actually verified.
81	
82	**Fixed:** all three fixtures now check the `CI` environment variable (which GitHub Actions sets unconditionally) before skipping. Unset locally (a developer's machine without a database configured) — unchanged behavior, still skips. Set in CI with the DSN still absent — `t.Fatalf`s, naming the exact missing variable, instead of skipping. Verified directly: ran each affected package's tests with `CI=true` and no DSN set, confirmed each one now fails loudly rather than silently passing.
83	
84	**Theo (04) is adding real Postgres/CockroachDB service containers to the CI workflow** so the DSNs are actually set going forward — this fix is what makes it impossible for that step to ever regress silently again: if a future workflow edit ever drops those service containers, the very next `pr-check` run fails loudly instead of quietly reverting to SQLite-only coverage with a green checkmark.

18	// requireOrSkipDSN reads envVar, skipping the test if unset when running
19	// locally (CI unset) but FAILING it (not skipping) if the CI env var is
20	// set and envVar is still unset — GitHub Actions sets CI=true
21	// unconditionally, so this is a reliable signal this test is running in
22	// the gate, not on a developer's machine. Naomi Voss's joint-review
23	// finding (originally caught in internal/dao/conformance, applying the
24	// same fix here since this file follows the identical pattern): a plain
25	// t.Skip made this integration suite look green in CI while never
26	// actually running against a real database — a skipped assertion that
27	// reads as a pass is worse than a red build (05's own §7 principle).
28	func requireOrSkipDSN(t *testing.T, envVar string) string {
29		t.Helper()
30		dsn := os.Getenv(envVar)
31		if dsn != "" {
32			return dsn
33		}
34		if os.Getenv("CI") != "" {
35			t.Fatalf("%s not set in CI — the issuer-database integration gate is not running", envVar)
36		}
37		t.Skipf("%s not set — skipping issuer-database integration test", envVar)
38		return ""
39	}
40	
41	// setupIssuerDB connects to TEST_POSTGRES_DSN — real Postgres
42	// verification is this file's responsibility, not something a
43	// hand-written fake could ever exercise. Applies the real
44	// migrations/issuer/00001_initial_schema.sql into a fresh, uniquely-named
45	// schema, and tears it down on cleanup.
46	//
47	// Applied through goose.Up (the pressly/goose/v3 library — the same
48	// parser and statement-splitting logic the real `goose` CLI 04's deploy
49	// pipeline runs uses), not a hand-rolled "extract the Up section and
50	// Exec it as one blob" approach. The two are NOT equivalent: goose's
51	// real statement splitter breaks a migration file apart at every
52	// semicolon unless a block is wrapped in "-- +goose StatementBegin"/
53	// "StatementEnd" markers, and a plain db.Exec of the whole Up section
54	// never exercises that splitting at all — it caught this file's own
55	// CREATE FUNCTION ... $$ ... $$ block cleanly in review here while the
56	// exact same file failed on the live cluster's real goose run
57	// (SQLSTATE 42601, "unterminated dollar-quoted string"). Running the
58	// actual migration runner is what "verification exercises the sequence
59	// the live system executes" (05-data-ops's own principle) means in
60	// practice, not just a stated intention.
61	//
62	// Do not add t.Parallel() to this file's tests without first addressing
63	// that goose.SetDialect/SetTableName below are package-level, global
64	// mutable state (goose's own API design, not a choice made here) — two
65	// tests running this setup concurrently would race on it (Nolan Reyes,
66	// PR #39 review). Safe today only because nothing in this file runs in
67	// parallel.
68	func setupIssuerDB(t *testing.T) *sql.DB {
69		t.Helper()
70		dsn := requireOrSkipDSN(t, "TEST_POSTGRES_DSN")
71	
72		db, err := sql.Open("pgx", dsn)
73		if err != nil {
74			t.Fatalf("opening TEST_POSTGRES_DSN: %v", err)
75		}
76		t.Cleanup(func() { _ = db.Close() })
77		db.SetMaxOpenConns(1)
78	
79		schemaName := fmt.Sprintf("test_issuer_%d", rand.Int63())
80		if _, err := db.Exec("CREATE SCHEMA " + schemaName); err != nil {
81			t.Fatalf("creating test schema: %v", err)
82		}
83		t.Cleanup(func() {
84			_, _ = db.Exec("DROP SCHEMA " + schemaName + " CASCADE")
85		})
86		if _, err := db.Exec("SET search_path TO " + schemaName); err != nil {
87			t.Fatalf("setting search_path: %v", err)
88		}
89	
90		wd, err := os.Getwd()
91		if err != nil {
92			t.Fatalf("getwd: %v", err)
93		}
94		repoRoot := filepath.Join(wd, "..", "..") // this package: <repoRoot>/internal/api
95		migrationDir := filepath.Join(repoRoot, "migrations", "issuer")
96	
97		if err := goose.SetDialect("postgres"); err != nil {
98			t.Fatalf("goose.SetDialect: %v", err)
99		}
100		// Matches the real deploy pipeline's `-table goose_db_version_issuer`
101		// invocation (deploy/manifests.yaml) — the version table lives inside
102		// this test's own schema via search_path, so it never collides with
103		// a real deployment's table of the same name.
104		goose.SetTableName("goose_db_version_issuer")
105		if err := goose.Up(db, migrationDir); err != nil {
106			t.Fatalf("applying issuer migration via goose.Up: %v", err)
107		}
108	
109		return db
110	}

112	func insertOAuthClient(t *testing.T, db *sql.DB, clientID, name string, secretHash *string, secretState string, scopes []string, audience, status string) {
113		t.Helper()
114		scopeLiteral := "{" + strings.Join(scopes, ",") + "}"
115		_, err := db.Exec(`
116			INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, client_secret_hash, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status)
117			VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, $6, $7)
118		`, clientID, name, secretHash, secretState, scopeLiteral, audience, status)
119		if err != nil {
120			t.Fatalf("inserting oauth_client row: %v", err)
121		}
122	}

124	func TestPostgresClientStore_Get_Found(t *testing.T) {
125		db := setupIssuerDB(t)
126		hash, err := hashSecret("s3cr3t")
127		if err != nil {
128			t.Fatalf("hashing secret: %v", err)
129		}
130		insertOAuthClient(t, db, "qa-review-client", "QA Review Client", &hash, "set", []string{"profile:search"}, "loginid-api-service", "active")
131	
132		store := NewPostgresClientStore(db)
133		rec, ok, err := store.Get(context.Background(), "qa-review-client")
134		if err != nil {
135			t.Fatalf("Get: %v", err)
136		}
137		if !ok {
138			t.Fatalf("Get returned ok=false for a row that exists")
139		}
140		if rec.Name != "QA Review Client" || rec.Audience != "loginid-api-service" || rec.GrantedScope != Scope("profile:search") || !rec.Active {
141			t.Errorf("unexpected record: %+v", rec)
142		}
143		if !verifySecret("s3cr3t", rec.ClientSecretHash) {
144			t.Errorf("stored hash doesn't verify against the secret it was created with")
145		}
146	}

199	// TestPostgresClientStore_UpdateSecretHash is the opportunistic-rehash
200	// mechanism's own storage-layer test (handoff-03-auth.md v7) — a real
201	// UPDATE against a real row, confirmed by re-reading it back, not just
202	// asserted from the SQL text.
203	func TestPostgresClientStore_UpdateSecretHash(t *testing.T) {
204		db := setupIssuerDB(t)
205		oldHash, err := hashSecret("s3cr3t")
206		if err != nil {
207			t.Fatalf("hashing secret: %v", err)
208		}
209		insertOAuthClient(t, db, "rehash-client", "Rehash Test", &oldHash, "set", []string{"profile:search"}, "aud", "active")
210	
211		newHash, err := hashSecret("s3cr3t")
212		if err != nil {
213			t.Fatalf("hashing secret: %v", err)
214		}
215		store := NewPostgresClientStore(db)
216		if err := store.UpdateSecretHash(context.Background(), "rehash-client", newHash); err != nil {
217			t.Fatalf("UpdateSecretHash: %v", err)
218		}
219	
220		rec, ok, err := store.Get(context.Background(), "rehash-client")
221		if err != nil || !ok {
222			t.Fatalf("Get after UpdateSecretHash: ok=%v err=%v", ok, err)
223		}
224		if rec.ClientSecretHash != newHash {
225			t.Errorf("stored hash = %q, want the new hash %q", rec.ClientSecretHash, newHash)
226		}
227		if !verifySecret("s3cr3t", rec.ClientSecretHash) {
228			t.Errorf("updated hash doesn't verify against the secret it was set to")
229		}
230	}

240	// TestOAuthClient_SchemaConstraints exercises the migration's own CHECK
241	// constraints directly against a real Postgres — the DDL is the actual
242	// enforcement site for these invariants, not application code, so the
243	// test belongs here rather than only asserted in prose.
244	func TestOAuthClient_SchemaConstraints(t *testing.T) {
245		db := setupIssuerDB(t)
246	
247		cases := []struct {
248			name string
249			sql  string
250			args []any
251		}{
252			{
253				name: "client_id too short fails the charset CHECK",
254				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ($1, 'x', 'none', '{profile:search}', 'aud', 'active')`,
255				args: []any{"short"},
256			},
257			{
258				name: "secret_state=set with NULL hash violates the tying CHECK",
259				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, client_secret_hash, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', NULL, 'set', '{profile:search}', 'aud', 'active')`,
260			},
261			{
262				name: "secret_state=none with a non-NULL hash violates the tying CHECK",
263				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, client_secret_hash, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'somehash', 'none', '{profile:search}', 'aud', 'active')`,
264			},
265			{
266				name: "multiple granted_scopes violates the single-scope CHECK",
267				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'none', '{profile:search,profile:read:any}', 'aud', 'active')`,
268			},
269			{
270				// array_length(x, 1) returns NULL (not 0) for an empty array,
271				// and a CHECK passes on NULL — this insert is exactly what
272				// the original (broken) CHECK text would have silently
273				// admitted (Priya Nandakumar's review, PR #34).
274				name: "empty granted_scopes violates the single-scope CHECK",
275				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'none', '{}', 'aud', 'active')`,
276			},
277			{
278				name: "a NULL granted_scopes element violates the single-scope CHECK",
279				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'none', ARRAY[NULL]::text[], 'aud', 'active')`,
280			},
281			{
282				name: "an empty-string granted_scopes element violates the single-scope CHECK",
283				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'none', ARRAY['']::text[], 'aud', 'active')`,
284			},
285			{
286				name: "unrecognized status violates the status enum CHECK",
287				sql:  `INSERT INTO oauth_client (client_id, name, secret_state, granted_scopes, audience, status) VALUES ('valid-client-id', 'x', 'none', '{profile:search}', 'aud', 'pending')`,
288			},
289		}
290		for _, c := range cases {
291			t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
292				_, err := db.Exec(c.sql, c.args...)
293				if err == nil {
294					t.Errorf("expected a CHECK-constraint violation, got no error")
295				}
296			})
297		}
298	}
299	
300	// TestOAuthClient_UpdatedAtTrigger proves the BEFORE UPDATE trigger
301	// actually advances updated_at — the enforcement site is the trigger
302	// firing on a real UPDATE, not the migration text alone (Priya
303	// Nandakumar's review, PR #34: this table has no application-code
304	// writing layer to rely on for setting updated_at, unlike the main DAO
305	// schema, so the trigger is the only thing that can't be forgotten).
306	func TestOAuthClient_UpdatedAtTrigger(t *testing.T) {
307		db := setupIssuerDB(t)
308		insertOAuthClient(t, db, "trigger-test-client", "Trigger Test", nil, "none", []string{"profile:search"}, "aud", "active")
309	
310		var createdAt, updatedAtBefore time.Time
311		if err := db.QueryRow(`SELECT created_at, updated_at FROM oauth_client WHERE client_id = $1`, "trigger-test-client").Scan(&createdAt, &updatedAtBefore); err != nil {
312			t.Fatalf("querying initial row: %v", err)
313		}
314		if !createdAt.Equal(updatedAtBefore) {
315			t.Fatalf("precondition failed: created_at (%v) and updated_at (%v) should start equal", createdAt, updatedAtBefore)
316		}
317	
318		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond) // ensure now() advances past the default's own now()
319		if _, err := db.Exec(`UPDATE oauth_client SET status = 'disabled' WHERE client_id = $1`, "trigger-test-client"); err != nil {
320			t.Fatalf("updating row: %v", err)
321		}
322	
323		var updatedAtAfter time.Time
324		if err := db.QueryRow(`SELECT updated_at FROM oauth_client WHERE client_id = $1`, "trigger-test-client").Scan(&updatedAtAfter); err != nil {
325			t.Fatalf("querying updated row: %v", err)
326		}
327		if !updatedAtAfter.After(updatedAtBefore) {
328			t.Errorf("updated_at did not advance after UPDATE: before=%v after=%v", updatedAtBefore, updatedAtAfter)
329		}
330	}
