result
1	# LT-41 refinement — S8: connector `/auth` + `/identity` (Q3)
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `03-engineering-delivery/backlog.md` S8 (LT-41); `02-ai-security-architecture/connector-security.md` (including its attack-tree table), `decisions/connector-token-lifecycle-redblue.md`, `handoff-03-auth.md` v6.
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one, now
8	
9	Q3 (the third assignment question) has no code yet, and it's genuinely independent of LT-51's in-flight work: the connector only *validates* a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token presented to it — it doesn't need to know how `api-service` obtains that token in the first place (that's LT-33, 02's still-open story, on `api-service`'s outbound side). Pulling this now keeps refinement from being the bottleneck while Renata and Theo build LT-51.
10	
11	**Checked the dependency graph rather than assuming:** LT-33 (02, `handoff-04-secrets.md` row 10 + `api-service`'s client credential) depends in part on "03's connector-client implementation," but that's the *outbound caller* inside `api-service`, not this story — this story is `cmd/idp-connector` itself, the inbound side. LT-41 can be built, refined, and merged without LT-33 closing first; LT-33 is what wires `api-service` to call it correctly, a separate piece.
12	
13	## Correction to `backlog.md`'s own S8 text, found before drafting
14	
15	**Three real controls from `connector-security.md`'s attack-tree table (S3 red/blue, already run and ruled) needed fixing relative to `backlog.md`'s S8 acceptance criteria — one truly missing, one named in prose but never promoted to a checkable criterion, one already fixed in this doc but not credited in this section (Desmond's claims check corrected all three of these framings).** The table is the authoritative, already-ratified source:
16	
17	1. **Leaf 1 (connection-reuse token leak) — named in prose, not promoted to a criterion.** `backlog.md`'s S8 **Description** paragraph already says, almost verbatim, "fresh isolated `Authorization` header per outbound request, never inherited from pooling/keep-alive" — so this isn't an undocumented gap, it's a requirement stated in prose that never became its own checkable acceptance criterion with an enforcement site. The criteria list only has the generic timeout/pool/backoff bullet. Promoted below.
18	2. **Leaf 5 (credential-stuffing oracle) — a genuine, total gap.** Rate limiting on outbound vendor calls, keyed per calling-API-client **and** per source IP, with a stricter threshold specifically on vendor-auth **failure ratio** (not just volume), paired with exponential backoff per identity attempted. Confirmed by reading `backlog.md`'s S8 text directly: no rate-limiting language anywhere, not even in prose.
19	3. **Leaf 4 (memory/crash-dump exposure), narrow-scoping half — already fixed in this doc's criteria below, just not credited here until now.** Two sub-controls beyond "never log the token" (which S8's existing criteria already cover): the token excluded from generic request/response dumps used in panic traces/debug output, and the token variable scoped to the narrowest function possible, never passed up the call stack. Both are already present as their own bullets below; this section previously undercounted the fix as covering two gaps instead of three.
20	
21	Both are added below as acceptance criteria, since the attack-tree table already ruled on them (Residual L on both, after blue's response) — this refinement is transcribing an already-ratified control, not inventing a new one.
22	
23	## Acceptance criteria (restated from `backlog.md` S8, plus the two additions above)
24	
25	- [ ] Implements 02's token-lifecycle spec (`connector-security.md`, `decisions/connector-token-lifecycle-redblue.md`) without re-litigating the no-cache-by-default posture.
26	- [ ] No vendor token is persisted or cached across requests by default; if a future exception is ever justified it requires re-review, not a silent default change — enforcement site: no storage/cache call in the token-fetch path.
27	- [ ] Every outbound vendor call sets `context.WithTimeout`; the outbound HTTP client uses a bounded connection pool; token-refresh retries back off rather than tight-looping — enforcement site: the HTTP client construction plus a test asserting a timeout fires.
28	- [ ] **Added (attack-tree leaf 1):** a fresh, isolated `Authorization` header is constructed per outbound vendor request — never inherited from HTTP connection/keep-alive reuse. Enforcement site: a test making two sequential vendor calls with two different presented tokens through the same pooled client, asserting the second call's outbound header matches its own token, not the first call's.
29	- [ ] **Added (attack-tree leaf 5):** outbound vendor calls are rate-limited keyed per calling-API-client identity **and** per source IP independently, with a stricter threshold on vendor-auth failure ratio specifically (not just raw volume), paired with exponential backoff per identity attempted. Enforcement site: the rate-limit/backoff component plus a test asserting a high-failure-ratio pattern trips the stricter threshold before a high-volume-but-mostly-successful pattern would.
30	- [ ] The connector's own `/auth`/`/identity` handlers validate an inbound JWT with `aud: idp-connector-service` and `scope: connector:identity-lookup`, distinct from `api-service`'s audience — enforcement site: this handler's own middleware instance, with a test asserting a `loginid-api-service`-audience token is rejected here.
31	- [ ] `/identity` accepts the vendor token as a request header (not a body field, since the assignment's fixed `{"phone","name"}` body has no credential field), fails closed with a generic error that doesn't distinguish "no token" from "bad token" from "vendor rejected it" in **response content** (same user-enumeration discipline as elsewhere in this project) — per `connector-security.md`'s F3 resolution.
32	- [ ] **Resolved by 02's ruling (Tobias's objection, routed to Marcus):** apply a uniform response-time floor (~100ms, well under any `context.WithTimeout` budget elsewhere in the chain) across all three failure branches (no-token, bad-token, vendor-rejected) — narrows the trivially-observable timing gap into something requiring statistical analysis over many requests, not full closure. **The remainder is an accepted residual risk, stated explicitly in `connector-security.md` §4, not engineered away further** — full closure (delaying every request to match the vendor's worst-case latency) would be a real availability cost disproportionate to what it protects, since what's disclosed even in the worst case is only that a presented token's shape reached the vendor, never any subject's identity, existence, or PII (§4 already normalizes vendor-rejection reasons so *which* check failed doesn't leak either). This is a different, and correctly different, resolution from LT-51's dummy-Argon2id fix: that leak was a fully-controlled local computation makeable genuinely constant-time; this one involves a third-party network call whose latency distribution isn't this connector's to control. Enforcement site: `connector-security.md` §4's stated floor and residual-risk language, plus a test asserting all three branches return within a bounded time delta of each other (not "both fast" or "both slow" — comparably close, per the floor).
33	- [ ] The token variable is scoped to the narrowest function possible and never passed up the call stack beyond the vendor-call function itself — per attack-tree leaf 4's control (accepted that Go gives no guaranteed memory zeroing, but the exposure window is narrowed to one function call, not eliminated).
34	- [ ] Never logs, in any environment or error path: the end-user's vendor password, any vendor `access_token` or full `Authorization` header value, or raw vendor error response bodies (per `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s never-log list) — enforcement site: log-call review against that list, plus confirming the token field is excluded from any generic request/response dump used in panic traces or debug output, not only application logs (leaf 4's "same discipline as application logs" extension).
35	
36	## Non-goals
37	
38	- No implementation of `api-service`'s outbound call to this connector, or how `api-service` obtains its own `connector:identity-lookup` token — that's LT-33 (02, still open), a separate story.
39	- No real ABC/XYZ vendor integration — a stubbed vendor per the assignment's generic framing; this story's job is the connector's own contract and security properties, not a specific vendor's API quirks.
40	- No change to `handoff-03-auth.md`'s scope vocabulary or claim scheme — already fixed (v6).
41	- No shared rate-limit store/edge mechanism — attack-tree leaf 5 names that as 04's mechanism if enforced at a gateway; this story's own in-process rate limiting is sufficient for this take-home's scale, matching the pattern already used for `RateLimiter`/`TouchCounter`/`GrantLimiter`.
42	
43	## Flagged gap for the joint LT-34/39/40/51 live-URL review script (not this story's own scope)
44	
45	**Per team-lead's request to update the joint review script** (mint a token, create a profile via whatever write path exists, search it masked, read it full with a `reason_code`, hit the 429 cap): **checked directly — there is no write/create path anywhere in `internal/api`.** `backlog.md`'s S6 (LT-39) only implements search/retrieve (Q2's explicit scope); no `POST /profiles` handler exists, and none is currently planned as a story. The migration `Job`'s `shared/` directory only seeds the `auth_method` lookup row (per `migration-approach.md`), not `user_profile`/`user_credential` test data — there's no seeding mechanism for a reviewable profile either.
46	
47	**This is a real gap in the joint review script's own plan, not something this story can quietly work around.** Two options, neither decided here: (a) a small, explicit "seed one test profile" step — either a one-off manual `INSERT` against the deployed Postgres instance (out-of-band, like the credential bootstraps) for review purposes only, clearly not a production data-entry path, or (b) a minimal `POST /profiles` handler as a new, small story if profile creation is expected to exist at all for a complete Q1/Q2 demonstration. Flagging both to team-lead rather than picking one — this is a product/scope decision (does the submission need a write path at all?), not an engineering detail.
48	
49	## PO review script
50	
51	**This connector has no public Ingress route by design** (`connector-security.md`: only `api-service` may call it) — "reviewable" for this story means two different things depending on the criterion, stated explicitly below rather than left to guess which method applies to which:
52	
53	**Verifiable by code/test review only (no cluster access needed):**
54	1. Confirm the fresh-header-per-request test (leaf 1) exists and passes.
55	2. Confirm the failure-ratio rate-limit test (leaf 5) exists and passes.
56	3. Confirm the token-variable narrow-scoping: read the vendor-call code path directly, confirm the token variable's scope doesn't escape the vendor-call function.
57	4. **The never-log criterion is test/log-review, not URL-reviewable at all** — confirm no log call anywhere references the token, the vendor password, or a raw vendor error body, by reading the code, not by trying to trigger and inspect a log line live.
58	5. Confirm the uniform-response-time-floor test (Marcus's ruling) exists and asserts all three `/identity` failure branches return within a bounded time delta of each other.
59	
60	**Verifiable only in-cluster (needs `kubectl` access, not the public URL):**
61	6. `kubectl -n loginid-takehome port-forward svc/idp-connector 8081:80`, then `POST localhost:8081/auth` and `POST localhost:8081/identity` directly, using a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped QA client credential (a fourth QA client, seeded by Theo alongside the other three — same secret-handling discipline: fetched via `kubectl get secret` at review time, never pasted into a doc/message/Jira).
62	7. Confirm the connector's inbound middleware rejects a `loginid-api-service`-audience token presented at this in-cluster endpoint (wrong audience for this service) — the actual audience-check enforcement, not just a unit test's assertion of the same logic.
63	8. Confirm `/identity`'s generic failure response doesn't distinguish no-token/bad-token/vendor-rejected cases when hit this way, and — per Marcus's ruling — that all three take comparable time (a rough manual timing check, not a load test).
64	
65	**Not part of the LT-34/39/40/51 joint review**, which covers Q1/Q2 only — this story's acceptance is its own separate pass, combining the code-review items above with the in-cluster port-forward check once PR #40 is ready.

68	
69	**Desmond Okafor — claims check:** Independently re-verified the "no write path exists" claim against `origin/main`'s actual `internal/app/verifier_router.go` — only `/healthz`, `POST /profiles/search`, `GET /profiles/{id}` registered; `CreateProfileWithCredential` appears only in test fakes, never called from a handler. Verified leaf 5's quote and "total gap" framing exactly. Corrected leaf 1's framing: the fresh-header requirement was already in `backlog.md`'s S8 *Description* prose, just never promoted to its own checkable acceptance criterion — not an undocumented gap, a documented-but-unenforceable one. Caught that leaf 4's narrow-scoping/no-dump-leak sub-controls were already fixed in this doc's criteria but not credited in the correction section — three gaps closed, not two. All fixes applied above.
70	
71	**Tobias Lindqvist — objection:** F3's identical-response-content requirement for `/identity`'s fail-closed error doesn't close a timing side-channel — the three failure branches (no-token, bad-token, vendor-rejected) have inherently different latencies, the last requiring an actual network round-trip to the vendor. Unlike LT-51's dummy-Argon2id case, this can't be cleanly equalized (one branch is a genuinely variable network call). Routed to Marcus for a ruling rather than deciding unilaterally — his own proposed floor is honestly partial, not a full close, which is exactly why this needs a security-owner decision on the trade-off, not a refinement-doc default.
72	
73	**Naomi Voss — ruling:** All fixes applied, including Marcus's ruling on the timing-oracle question (a ~100ms uniform floor, remainder accepted as a named residual risk in `connector-security.md` §4 — a correctly different resolution from LT-51's fully-closable case, not a half-measure). This refinement is now fully resolved and accepted, ready for Renata.

79	## PO in-cluster review — blocking defect found, 2026-09-13 (build `3888b14`)
80	
81	**Reviewer:** Naomi Voss (PO). `kubectl -n loginid-takehome port-forward svc/idp-connector 8081:443`, confirmed `/healthz` reachable and build-matched, then minted a genuinely valid `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token (correct `aud: idp-connector-service`, correct scope) via the fourth QA client and presented it to `POST /auth` and `POST /identity`.
82	
83	**Blocking defect, confirmed from the connector's own logs, not inferred:** every request — the genuinely valid token included, indistinguishable from the no-token and wrong-audience cases — returned 401. Logs: `"JWKS fetch failed (host=, class=network_error): api: fetching JWKS from : Get \"\": unsupported protocol scheme \"\""`. Root cause confirmed in the manifest: `idp-connector`'s `Deployment` in `deploy/manifests.yaml` sets only `HTTP_ADDR` — `AUTH_JWKS_URL` is never set, unlike `api-service.yaml`, which correctly sets it to the issuer's in-cluster JWKS endpoint. The connector's inbound auth is completely non-functional as deployed — not a partial gap, a total one, for every caller. Same class of defect as the earlier `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER`/`AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE` gap (config surface supported by code, never wired into this specific manifest).
84	
85	**Wider than first reported — team-lead's own check found a second missing variable.** The connector's Deployment sets only `HTTP_ADDR` and `CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE` — `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER` is missing as well as `AUTH_JWKS_URL`. Also, my own suggested fix value was wrong in one detail: the JWKS URL needs the issuer Service's actual `:443` port explicit (the same lesson from PR #43's earlier fix), not the bare hostname I proposed.
86	
87	**Durable fix, not just a one-line patch:** Theo adds both missing env vars with the correct port. Renata makes the connector binary refuse to start with either empty (the same fail-loud discipline already added to `api-service` after the earlier issuer/audience gap). +## PO in-cluster review — blocking defect found, 2026-09-13 (build `3888b14`)
+
+**Reviewer:** Naomi Voss (PO). `kubectl -n loginid-takehome port-forward svc/idp-connector 8081:443`, confirmed `/healthz` reachable and build-matched, then minted a genuinely valid `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token (correct `aud: idp-connector-service`, correct scope) via the fourth QA client and presented it to `POST /auth` and `POST /identity`.
+
+**Blocking defect, confirmed from the connector's own logs, not inferred:** every request — the genuinely valid token included, indistinguishable from the no-token and wrong-audience cases — returned 401. Logs: `"JWKS fetch failed (host=, class=network_error): api: fetching JWKS from : Get \"\": unsupported protocol scheme \"\""`. Root cause confirmed in the manifest: `idp-connector`'s `Deployment` in `deploy/manifests.yaml` sets only `HTTP_ADDR` — `AUTH_JWKS_URL` is never set, unlike `api-service.yaml`, which correctly sets it to the issuer's in-cluster JWKS endpoint. The connector's inbound auth is completely non-functional as deployed — not a partial gap, a total one, for every caller. Same class of defect as the earlier `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER`/`AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE` gap (config surface supported by code, never wired into this specific manifest).
+
+**Wider than first reported — team-lead's own check found a second missing variable.** The connector's Deployment sets only `HTTP_ADDR` and `CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE` — `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER` is missing as well as `AUTH_JWKS_URL`. Also, my own suggested fix value was wrong in one detail: the JWKS URL needs the issuer Service's actual `:443` port explicit (the same lesson from PR #43's earlier fix), not the bare hostname I proposed.
+
+**Durable fix, not just a one-line patch:** Theo adds both missing env vars with the correct port. Renata makes the connector binary refuse to start with either empty (the same fail-loud discipline already added to `api-service` after the earlier issuer/audience gap). Renata also adds a CI test that diffs each Deployment's actual env against what its `APP_MODE`/binary mode requires — a structural check meant to stop this exact class of gap (config supported by code, never wired into one specific manifest) from recurring a fourth time.
+
+**Process note, per team-lead:** this is the third enforcement-site gap of this kind found across the joint review (after `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER`/`AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE` on `api-service`, and the rate-limit/replica gap), and the first one caught by PO review rather than PM pre-review — the process catching it at the layer it's supposed to, not a sign the earlier two passes were careless.
+
+**Verdict: BLOCKED, holding for redeploy.** Cannot meaningfully test audience-rejection, `/identity`'s fail-closed error content, or the timing-floor mitigation when the baseline valid-token case doesn't work at all. LT-41 goes back to In Dev now, returns to In Test when the fix deploys — team-lead will send the build SHA.

+## PO in-cluster review — resumed and passed, 2026-09-13 (fix confirmed live)
+
+**Reviewer:** Naomi Voss (PO). **Note on build SHA:** team-lead reported build `f8787fa`; the live pod I actually port-forwarded to reports `ebce65a9e1c8935815e25bad230c59c7c60ca7d0`. Before assuming staleness, I checked the pod's actual live env directly (`kubectl get pod ... -o jsonpath='{.spec.containers[0].env}'`) — both `AUTH_JWKS_URL` (with the correct `:443` port) and `AUTH_JWT_ISSUER` are present and correct on the running pod regardless of which SHA is authoritative. The fix is live; recording the discrepancy honestly rather than either silently correcting it or blocking on it.
+
+**Full script run, port-forwarded (`kubectl -n loginid-takehome port-forward svc/idp-connector 8081:443`), fresh connector-scoped token minted directly via `kubectl get secret`/`/auth/token`:**
+
+- `POST /auth` with a valid connector token and the stub vendor's real seeded credentials (`demo-user`/`demo-password`) — **200**, valid vendor `access_token` returned. JWKS verification now genuinely works (previously every request, valid or not, failed identically).
+- `POST /identity` with that vendor token as `X-Vendor-Access-Token` — **200**, full identity record, matching the stub's seeded response. **Happy path confirmed.**
+- `POST /identity`, connector token but no vendor token — **401**, generic `{"error":"identity_lookup_failed"}**.
+- `POST /identity`, connector token + a garbage vendor token — **401**, same generic error shape — confirms F3's identical-content requirement across both failure branches.
+- `POST /auth` with a wrong-audience token (the `loginid-api-service` search token from the joint review) — **401**, correctly rejected — the connector's own audience check (`aud: idp-connector-service`) works.
+- `POST /auth`/`/identity` with no token at all — **401**, correctly rejected (fast, ~40ms — this is the authn-middleware layer, not one of the three `/identity`-internal branches the timing floor targets).
+- **Timing floor (Marcus's ruling):** the two genuinely comparable `/identity`-internal failure branches (no vendor token: ~137ms; bad vendor token: ~220ms) both landed well above the near-instant authn-layer rejection (~40ms), consistent with the ~100ms floor being applied — not perfectly identical (137 vs. 220ms), which matches Marcus's own framing that this narrows the timing gap rather than fully closes it, an accepted residual, not a defect.
+- **A live bonus confirmation, not originally scripted:** repeated failed-vendor-auth attempts in quick succession triggered a real `429 {"error":"slow_down"}` from the connector itself — attack-tree leaf 5's rate-limit/backoff-on-failure-ratio control, observed actually firing against a live failure pattern, not just unit-tested.
+- **Never-log criterion — confirmed directly from live logs** (`kubectl logs -l app=idp-connector`): every log line is a structured audit entry (`Sub`/`Scope`/`Kind`/`ReasonCode`/`Timestamp`) or a startup message — no vendor password, no vendor access token, no raw vendor error body, anywhere in the log stream across this entire test session.
+
+**Verdict: LT-41 PASSES.** Every criterion checkable in-cluster is confirmed live: happy paths, audience rejection, identical fail-closed content, the timing floor (as an accepted partial mitigation, per 02's own ruling), the failure-ratio rate limit actually firing, and the never-log discipline. **LT-41: Done.**

Run against the manifests as they stand today (post-PR-#48), it passes: this is now a live regression guard, catching a fourth occurrence of this exact defect class before it ever reaches a live pod, rather than a red test waiting on a manifest fix that hadn't landed yet. This is meant as a structural CI regression guard so this exact class of gap (config supported by code, never wired into one specific manifest) can't recur silently a fourth time.\n\nPlease do a real second-pass review — your usual detail-hawk focus on error semantics, nullability, and off-by-one issues, but also specifically:\n- Whether `RequiredAuthEnvVars`'s switch statement correctly covers every (service, mode) combination that actually exists in the manifests, and what happens on an unrecognized combination (currently returns nil — is that the right default, or does it risk silently checking nothing for a typo'd mode?).\n- Whether the manifest test's `serviceForImage` substring-matching on container image strings is robust, or could misfire (e.g., matching \"istio/idp-connector:sidecar\" as idp-connector, or partial substring collisions).\n- Whether `cmd/idp-connector/main.go`'s new validateAuthConfig is called at the right point in main() — before or after other initialization that might itself depend on these vars being set (i.e., does anything execute using a bad/empty AUTH_JWKS_URL etc. before the check fires?).\n- Whether cmd/api-service/main_test.go and cmd/idp-connector/main_test.go's t.Setenv-based tests correctly isolate from each other and from a real environment (no leakage risk).\n- General Go idiom and error-message quality.\n\nRead the actual diff (gh pr diff 53, or check out the branch) rather than trusting this description. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note> <result>Findings for Renata — PR #53, `fix/idp-connector-config-validation`: 1. **`config.go`: `RequiredAuthEnvVars` default case is the weakest point in this PR.** `internal/config/config.go` (new func, `default: return nil`) means any unrecognized `(service, mode)` pair — a typo'd `APP_MODE` like `"Verifier"` or `"verifer"` in a future manifest edit — silently requires *zero* env vars. Both `validateAuthConfig` call sites and the new manifest test read this same list, so a typo produces no startup failure and no CI failure: exactly the "config supported by code, never wired in" class of bug this PR exists to catch, just moved one level up. The default should either `panic`/return a sentinel "unrecognized" error the caller treats as fatal, or the manifest test should independently assert the *set* of `(service, mode)` pairs it saw is a subset of ones the switch explicitly recognizes (not just `len(required) == 0` → skip, which is what `manifest_test.go` does today at the `if len(required) == 0 { continue }` line — that line can't distinguish "issuer mode legitimately needs nothing extra" from "mode string didn't match anything"). 2. **`manifest_test.go`'s `serviceForImage` substring match will silently stop matching on a digest-pinned image.** It matches on `"/idp-connector:"` / `"/api-service:"` (colon required). A future manifest pinning by digest (`.../idp-connector@sha256:...`) has no such colon and would be `recognized=false`, silently skipped — not caught by the `checked == 0` guard since other deployments still match. Worth a comment or a second match arm for `@sha256:` at minimum, since digest-pinning is a plausible supply-chain hardening move 04 could make later without knowing it silences this test. 3. `serviceForImage`'s false-positive risk you asked about (e.g. `istio/idp-connector:sidecar`) is real in principle but not reachable today — no sidecars in either manifest — so I'd leave it, not block on it; flag as a known limitation in the doc comment. 4. **Call order in `cmd/idp-connector/main.go` is correct** — `validateAuthConfig()` (line 43) runs immediately after `config.Load()` and before the vendor client (`NewHTTPVendorClient`/stub) and `http.ListenAndServe` are constructed, so nothing executes against a bad/empty `AUTH_JWKS_URL` first. 5. **`t.Setenv` isolation is fine** in both `main_test.go` files — each subtest gets its own `t`, `t.Setenv` auto-restores at test end, no `t.Parallel()` used anywhere so no cross-subtest race. 6. Error messages are good — name the exact missing var, consistent with the "loud crash beats silent 401" framing stated in the comments. 1	// Package config loads the env-var configuration surface published in root
2	// PLANNING.md's Service boundaries section. Both cmd/api-service and
3	// cmd/idp-connector load through this package so the surface is defined once.
4	package config
5

9		"strings"

11	
12	// Config holds every environment variable named in PLANNING.md's Service
13	// boundaries section. Fields unused by S1's stub handlers are still loaded
14	// here so later stories don't have to touch this package's shape again.
15	type Config struct {
16		AppMode  string // "verifier" (default) | "issuer" — see LT-32
17		HTTPAddr string
18	
19		// JWTSigningKeyPath is the file path to the JWT signing private key
20		// (handoff-04-secrets.md row #2 — "runtime-injected as a file, never
21		// an env var"). Only meaningful when AppMode == "issuer"; empty in
22		// verifier mode, since the verifying Deployment's pod spec never
23		// mounts this Secret at all (LT-32's physical-absence criterion) and
24		// therefore has no path set to point at.
25		JWTSigningKeyPath string
26

28		DBDSN    string // resolved value: DB_DSN_FILE content takes precedence over DB_DSN
29	
30		AuthJWTIssuer   string
31		AuthJWTAudience string
32		// AuthJWKSURL is the verifier's only path to public key material
33		// (LT-40, handoff-03-auth.md v4): the issuer Deployment's in-cluster
34		// ClusterIP Service JWKS endpoint, e.g.
35		// "http://api-service-issuer.loginid-takehome.svc.cluster.local/.well-known/jwks.json".
36		// Never a mounted key file — JWKS survives rotation without a config
37		// change, since the verifier fetches by kid.
38		AuthJWKSURL string
39	
40		// AuthzQASub and AuthzQAAllowedProfileID seed the temporary,
41		// default-deny StopgapAuthorizer (internal/api) for LT-40/LT-51's
42		// joint live-URL review only — see StopgapAuthorizer's own doc
43		// comment for why a permissive default was explicitly ruled out.
44		// Both empty (the default) means the stand-in denies every
45		// profile:read:own request, the safe default until LT-51 seeds a
46		// real QA client credential and this is set to match it.
47		AuthzQASub              string

53		// a distinct database so a cross-database join to user_profile is
54		// structurally impossible), holding only oauth_client. Resolved the

83	// Load reads the process environment into a Config. DB_DSN_FILE, when set,
84	// is read and its trimmed content used as DBDSN in preference to DB_DSN —
85	// DB_DSN is a transitional fallback, not a permanent equal option, per
86	// handoff-04-secrets.md's S7 revision.
87	//
88	// APP_MODE is validated, not merely defaulted: an unrecognized value is a
89	// startup error rather than a silent fall-through to "verifier", because a
90	// typo'd issuer Deployment silently running as a verifier (or vice versa)
91	// is exactly the class of misconfiguration LT-32 exists to make loud
92	// instead of invisible.
93	//
94	// In issuer mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must be set and the file it names
95	// must actually be readable, checked here at startup rather than deferred
96	// to whatever first tries to use it. This is LT-32's defense-in-depth
97	// criterion: the verifying Deployment's pod spec never mounts this Secret
98	// at all (enforced on 04's side, by physical absence, not by this check),
99	// so if APP_MODE were ever misconfigured to "issuer" on that Deployment,
100	// this still fails startup rather than serving traffic in a half-issuer
101	// state with no actual path to the private key.
102	func Load() (Config, error) {
103		dsn, err := resolveDSNFrom("DB_DSN_FILE", "DB_DSN")
104		if err != nil {
105			return Config{}, err
106		}
107	
108		issuerDSN, err := resolveDSNFrom("ISSUER_DB_DSN_FILE", "ISSUER_DB_DSN")
109		if err != nil {
110			return Config{}, err
111		}
112	
113		appMode := os.Getenv("APP_MODE")
114		if appMode == "" {
115			appMode = "verifier"
116		}
117		if appMode != "verifier" && appMode != "issuer" {
118			return Config{}, fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE %q is not one of \"verifier\", \"issuer\"", appMode)
119		}
120	
121		signingKeyPath, err := resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode)
122		if err != nil {
123			return Config{}, err
124		}
125	
126		return Config{
127			AppMode:           appMode,
128			JWTSigningKeyPath: signingKeyPath,
129			HTTPAddr:          os.Getenv("HTTP_ADDR"),
130	
131			DBDriver: os.Getenv("DB_DRIVER"),
132			DBDSN:    dsn,
133	
134			IssuerDBDSN: issuerDSN,
135	
136			AuthJWTIssuer:   os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_ISSUER"),
137			AuthJWTAudience: os.Getenv("AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
138			AuthJWKSURL:     os.Getenv("AUTH_JWKS_URL"),
139	
140			AuthzQASub:              os.Getenv("AUTHZ_QA_SUB"),
141			AuthzQAAllowedProfileID: os.Getenv("AUTHZ_QA_ALLOWED_PROFILE_ID"),
142	
143			ConnectorJWTAudience:  os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE"),
144			ConnectorClientID:     os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID"),
145			ConnectorClientSecret: os.Getenv("CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET"),
146	
147			IDPABCBaseURL:      os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_BASE_URL"),
148			IDPABCClientID:     os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_ID"),
149			IDPABCClientSecret: os.Getenv("IDP_ABC_CLIENT_SECRET"),
150		}, nil
151	}
152	
153	// resolveSigningKeyPath enforces LT-32's issuer-mode requirement: in
154	// "issuer" mode, JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE must name a regular file this
155	// process can actually read at least one byte of, checked now rather than
156	// left for whatever code first tries to sign a token — a startup failure
157	// here is the only thing that distinguishes "issuer mode with no key"
158	// from "issuer mode with a key", and the whole point of the
159	// physical-absence criterion is that those two must never be confused for
160	// one another. In "verifier" mode the variable is not read at all: the
161	// verifying binary has no legitimate use for a signing-key path, set or
162	// not.
163	//
164	// This checks openable-and-non-empty at startup, not "will still be a
165	// valid key when S7 reads it" — a Secret rotated to empty or a volume
166	// re-provisioned later isn't something a one-time startup check can see
167	// (Nolan Reyes, PR #18 review). That's S7's problem to solve when it
168	// exists, not this story's: LT-32 only owns making a *missing* key loud
169	// at startup, not monitoring a *present* key's ongoing validity.
170	func resolveSigningKeyPath(appMode string) (string, error) {
171		if appMode != "issuer" {
172			return "", nil
173		}
174		path := os.Getenv("JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE")
175		if path == "" {
176			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer requires JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE to be set")
177		}
178		fi, err := os.Stat(path)
179		if err != nil {
180			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not accessible: %w", path, err)
181		}
182		// os.Open succeeds on a directory as readily as on a regular file —
183		// no error, nothing distinguishing the two. JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE
184		// pointed at a mount directory instead of the file inside it (an easy
185		// Kubernetes subPath-vs-mount-root mistake, and exactly the class of
186		// misconfiguration this story exists to catch) would otherwise pass
187		// this check and only fail later, whenever S7's signing code first
188		// tries to read it — deferring the failure this check exists to make
189		// immediate (Oren Castellan, PR #18 review).
190		if fi.IsDir() {
191			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is a directory, not a file", path)
192		}
193		f, err := os.Open(path)
194		if err != nil {
195			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is not readable: %w", path, err)
196		}
197		defer func() { _ = f.Close() }()
198		// Read one byte rather than open-close: an empty file (a Secret
199		// mounted but never populated) opens without error and would
200		// otherwise pass silently.
201		var buf [1]byte
202		if _, err := f.Read(buf[:]); err != nil {
203			return "", fmt.Errorf("config: APP_MODE=issuer set but JWT_SIGNING_KEY_FILE %q is empty or unreadable: %w", path, err)
204		}
205		return path, nil
206	}
207	
208	// resolveDSNFrom implements the DB_DSN_FILE-takes-precedence-over-DB_DSN
209	// pattern generically, parameterized by env var name so it serves both
210	// the main DAO's DSN and IssuerDBDSN without duplicating the logic.
211	func resolveDSNFrom(fileEnv, plainEnv string) (string, error) {
212		if path := os.Getenv(fileEnv); path != "" {
213			b, err := os.ReadFile(path)
214			if err != nil {
215				return "", fmt.Errorf("config: reading %s %q: %w", fileEnv, path, err)
216			}
217			return strings.TrimSpace(string(b)), nil
218		}
219		return os.Getenv(plainEnv), nil
220	}
221	
222	// RequiredAuthEnvVars is the single source of truth for which auth-related
223	// env vars a given (service, mode) combination MUST have set to function
224	// correctly — read by both this process's own startup validation
225	// (cmd/api-service/main.go's validateAuthConfig, cmd/idp-connector/main.go's
226	// equivalent) and, independently, by internal/config's own
227	// TestManifestsSetRequiredAuthEnvVars, which parses the actual deployed
228	// manifests (deploy/api-service.yaml, deploy/manifests.yaml) and asserts
229	// every Deployment sets everything its own (service, mode) requires.
230	//
231	// Exists because of two separate live-review incidents in one day, both
232	// the same shape: a real, correctly-signed token failed verification
233	// because a required auth env var was silently absent from a
234	// Deployment's manifest, and nothing caught it before a human noticed
235	// the symptom in production. cmd/api-service/main.go's own
236	// validateAuthConfig (added after the first incident) only checks the
237	// binary it lives in — it has no way to know cmd/idp-connector's
238	// Deployment was missing AUTH_JWKS_URL/AUTH_JWT_ISSUER entirely (the
239	// second incident), because idp-connector never runs that check at all
240	// until this same list is wired into its own main() too. And neither
241	// check, however complete, can catch a manifest that's simply missing
242	// the variable outright before a real request exercises the gap — only
243	// a test that reads the manifest text itself can do that ahead of time,
244	// which is what this declaration exists to make possible.
245	//
246	// service is "api-service" or "idp-connector" — the binary, not the
247	// Deployment name (api-service and api-service-issuer are the SAME
248	// binary in different modes). mode is "verifier"/"issuer" for
249	// api-service (matching AppMode's own two values), or "" for
250	// idp-connector, which has no mode concept.
251	func RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode string) []string {
252		switch {
253		case service == "api-service" && mode == "verifier":
254			return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER", "AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE", "AUTH_JWKS_URL"}
255		case service == "api-service" && mode == "issuer":
256			// AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE deliberately absent here — the issuer mints
257			// each token's aud from the per-client oauth_client.audience
258			// column (tokenhandler.go), never from its own config, so
259			// requiring it would reject a valid issuer configuration for a
260			// variable it has no use for (validateAuthConfig's own doc
261			// comment states this same reasoning).
262			return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER"}
263		case service == "idp-connector":
264			return []string{"AUTH_JWT_ISSUER", "AUTH_JWKS_URL", "CONNECTOR_JWT_AUDIENCE"}
265		default:
266			return nil
267		}
268	}

46	// validateAuthConfig catches exactly the two live-review findings that
47	// motivated it — first AUTH_JWT_ISSUER/AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE, then
48	// AUTH_JWKS_URL, silently absent from the deployed manifests, each time
49	// letting a verifier or issuer come up cleanly and fail in a way that
50	// looked like an ordinary token-rejection rather than a configuration
51	// gap. A verifier that quietly 401s everything, or an issuer that mints
52	// structurally-incomplete tokens, is worse than a crash-loop that names
53	// the exact missing variable.
54	//
55	// Reads config.RequiredAuthEnvVars(service, mode) — the single source of
56	// truth also read by internal/config's own manifest test — rather than
57	// hardcoding its own list, so this check and that test can never drift
58	// apart from each other. Checks the raw environment directly (not cfg's
59	// already-loaded fields): the manifest test checks for the same env var
60	// NAMES in the manifest text, so both sides operate on the identical
61	// primitive.
62	//
63	// Kept out of internal/config's shared Load() because AUTH_JWT_AUDIENCE
64	// is meaningless to cmd/idp-connector (which has its own
65	// ConnectorJWTAudience instead) and a blanket check in the shared loader
66	// would wrongly fail that binary's startup too — idp-connector runs the
67	// equivalent check itself, against its own required list.
68	func validateAuthConfig(mode app.Mode) error {
69		for _, name := range config.RequiredAuthEnvVars("api-service", string(mode)) {
70			if os.Getenv(name) == "" {
71				return fmt.Errorf("%s must be set in %s mode", name, mode)
72			}
73		}
74		return nil
75	}
76

78		cfg, err := config.Load()
79		if err != nil {

result
1	# LT-33 refinement — wire `api-service`'s client credential to call the connector, and the vendor-token pass-through
2	
3	**Product Owner:** Naomi Voss (01). **Research team:** Imogen Hale, Desmond Okafor, Tobias Lindqvist. **Engineering:** Renata Cole (03). **Source:** `02-ai-security-architecture/backlog.md` S13 (LT-33) — a 02-authored story whose implementation is 03's code, refined here since it's engineering work, same pattern as LT-51.
4	
5	**Who did the work:** Refined by Naomi Voss (PO); claims check Desmond Okafor; objection Tobias Lindqvist; engineering counterpart Renata Cole.
6	
7	## Why this one, now
8	
9	Per team-lead: this is the only story that makes Q2 and Q3 one system — everything up to now proves `api-service`'s own REST surface (Q2) and `idp-connector`'s own inbound contract (Q3) work in isolation, but nothing yet has `api-service` actually call the connector as part of a real flow. It has a real security surface (a client credential `api-service` itself must hold and use correctly), which is why 02 authored the story even though 03 writes the code.
10	
11	**Checked before drafting: this is genuinely greenfield.** `grep`'d `internal/api` and `cmd/api-service` for any existing connector-calling code — only the `ScopeConnectorIdentityLookup` constant exists (used by the connector's own inbound-audience check, already built and tested in LT-41). No outbound client, no onboarding-flow handler, nothing in `api-service` mints or uses a `connector:identity-lookup` token today.
12	
13	## What this story actually is
14	
15	Two related gaps `connector-security.md` and `handoff-04-secrets.md` already resolved at the design level; this story is the implementation:
16	
17	1. **`api-service` needs its own client credential** (row 10 of `handoff-04-secrets.md`) to authenticate to the connector's inbound auth in the first place — the same `POST /auth/token` mechanism LT-51 built, but `api-service` is itself a *client* of that endpoint here, not the resource server.
18	2. **The vendor-token pass-through** (`connector-security.md` F3): our own `/auth` returns the vendor's `access_token` to the authenticated internal caller (`api-service`, on the onboarding flow's behalf); `api-service` holds it only for the span between that call and the following `/identity` call, then presents it as a request header (`X-Vendor-Access-Token`, confirmed by name in LT-41's implementation) — never in the body, never persisted, never logged.
19	
20	## Acceptance criteria (from `handoff-04-secrets.md`/`connector-security.md`, restated for implementation)
21	
22	- [ ] `api-service` obtains a `connector:identity-lookup`-scoped token from `/auth/token` using its own client credential (`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET`, per `PLANNING.md`'s config surface — already named there, never implemented) before calling `idp-connector`'s `/auth` or `/identity`.
23	- [ ] **Credential holding/rotation (Marcus's ruling — nothing new, points to an existing row rather than re-specifying it):** `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_ID`/`CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` follow `handoff-04-secrets.md` row 10 exactly — file-mounted secret, `api-service`'s own ServiceAccount, single active secret per client (reissue generates a new one and immediately invalidates the old, no dual-secret overlap window at this scale). No new design here.
24	- [ ] **Failure mode when the connector is unreachable (Marcus's ruling):** bounded `context.WithTimeout` on the call, a small number of retries with backoff (itself rate-limited independent of any user-triggered retry, so a caller can't abuse it to hammer the connector), then a generic failure surfaced upward ("identity verification temporarily unavailable") — never internal error detail, never a hint at whether the failure is auth/network/vendor-side. **Never falls back to cached or stale identity data** — the same circuit-breaking discipline `connector-security.md` §4 already requires on the connector's own side, applied with equal force here. `api-service` failing to obtain its *own* connector token in the first place (a credential/config problem) is logged/alerted as a distinct failure class from "got a token fine, connector unreachable" (an availability problem) — the former is a config/secret issue, not ordinary downtime.
25	- [ ] The onboarding flow's vendor-auth step (whatever handler mediates the two-screen flow `ux-notes.md` describes) receives the vendor `access_token` from our `/auth` response and holds it **only** for the span between that call and the following `/identity` call — enforcement site: the handler/service layer that mediates this, not the connector (which stays stateless, per LT-41). No storage, no cache, scoped to the narrowest function possible — same discipline `connector-security.md`'s attack-tree leaf 4 already requires on the connector's own side, applied symmetrically here since `api-service` now holds the same class of secret for the same short window.
26	- [ ] `/identity` is called with the vendor token as a request header (`X-Vendor-Access-Token`, matching LT-41's already-built expectation), never a body field.
27	- [ ] **Corrected per Marcus's (02) ruling — the opposite of this criterion's original draft.** `api-service`'s own `connector:identity-lookup` token is a client's own OAuth2 client-credentials access token, not a per-end-user session token — a genuinely different case from the vendor token (which belongs to a specific end-user's onboarding flow and is correctly never cached per `connector-security.md`'s S3 ruling). Standard client-credentials practice, and the correct one here: `api-service` **caches its own connector-scoped token in-memory and reuses it across calls until near expiry**, refreshing shortly before TTL or on a 401 indicating early expiry — not re-minting fresh before every single call. Per-replica in-memory caching only, no shared store needed (multiple valid tokens for the same `client_id` can coexist; independent per-replica authentication is normal for this grant type). Never logged, never persisted to disk — same discipline as every other JWT in this project.
28	- [ ] Never logs, in any environment or error path: the vendor password, the vendor `access_token`, `api-service`'s own `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET`, or any full `Authorization`/`X-Vendor-Access-Token` header value — per `handoff-04-secrets.md`'s never-log list, same standard already enforced on the connector's own side (LT-41).
29	- [ ] `01-product-industry-research-design/ux-notes.md`'s two-screen flow description and this mechanism agree on what the frontend/onboarding-mediating layer actually holds between screens — checked directly: `ux-notes.md:27` already states the token is "held only by this onboarding flow, only for the span between the two calls, and is never persisted past this signup step" (added per Marcus's exact sentence during the earlier consistency-pass fix) — this criterion is **already satisfied by the existing doc**, no coordination needed unless the actual implementation ends up diverging from that description.
30	- [ ] **Resolved by inspection, per Tobias's objection — a one-line confirmation, not new work.** LT-33 is the first story that gives LT-41's connector rate-limiting a real caller — once wired, every onboarding attempt shares the same `api-service` client identity and (in-cluster) a small, stable set of source IPs, so if the connector's rate-limiting were keyed only on caller-identity+IP (as `backlog.md`'s S8 text reads in isolation), it couldn't distinguish one legitimate user's retry from an attacker enumerating many different people's `name`/`phone` pairs through the same caller. **Checked directly against the actual implementation, not assumed:** `internal/connector/identitylimiter.go`'s `identityRateLimiter` (a distinct component from the caller/IP-keyed `vendorLimiter`, both already built in LT-41) keys on `hashIdentity(callerSub, phone, name)` — the vendor-identity being looked up is already part of the rate-limit key, not just the caller. This means the concern Tobias raised is already correctly resolved by existing code: distinct onboarding attempts (different `name`/`phone` pairs) from the same `api-service` caller get separate buckets, bounded by a capped tracked-identity table (`maxTrackedIdentities`) with a shared, deliberately-stricter overflow bucket once that cap is hit (Marcus's ruling, PR #40 — "fail-toward-stricter under pressure, not fail-open"). **This story's only obligation here is to confirm `api-service` actually passes real, distinct `name`/`phone` values through per onboarding attempt** (trivially true — that's the whole point of the call) — no new correlation-ID mechanism is needed, since the connector's own identity-keyed limiter already does this job.
31	
32	## Non-goals
33	
34	- No change to `idp-connector`'s own inbound handlers — already built and tested (LT-41); this story only calls them correctly from the other side.
35	- No change to the token-issuance mechanism itself — `/auth/token` already exists (LT-51); `api-service` is a new *caller* of it, not a change to how it works.
36	- No real vendor UI/frontend — the "onboarding flow" here is `api-service`'s own handler/service-layer mediation, not a client-facing UI (out of scope for this backend-focused assignment, per `01-product-industry-research-design`'s track boundary).
37	- No new client-provisioning UI — `api-service`'s own connector-calling credential is provisioned the same out-of-band way as every other credential in this project (QA clients, signing key, DB credentials).
38	
39	## PO review script
40	
41	1. **Corrected per Marcus's ruling:** confirm `api-service` caches and reuses its own connector-scoped token in-memory across calls until near expiry (refreshing before TTL or on a 401), **not** minting fresh per onboarding flow — read the code directly.
42	2. Confirm the vendor token's variable scope doesn't escape the mediating handler function — same check applied to the connector's own code in LT-41.
43	3. Confirm `/identity` is called with the header, not the body — a test asserting the outbound request shape.
44	4. Confirm no log call anywhere references the vendor token, vendor password, or `CONNECTOR_CLIENT_SECRET` — log-call review against the never-log list.
45	5. Confirm the connector-unreachable failure path: bounded timeout, rate-limited backoff, generic upward failure with no internal detail, no fallback to stale/cached identity data.
46	6. Confirm own-token-acquisition failures (credential/config problem) are logged/alerted as a distinct class from connector-unreachable failures (availability problem) — not lumped together.
47	7. Once deployed: this is testable end-to-end only via the actual onboarding flow, which isn't a public-URL-exposed endpoint on its own (no assignment question asks for one) — acceptance is code-review plus a unit/integration test exercising the mediating handler against a fake connector client, not a live-URL check.
48	
49	## Research team review
50	
51	**Desmond Okafor — claims check:** Verified all three flagged items exactly: the `connector-security.md` F3 quotes are accurate (checked §1 and the attack-tree table directly), the "genuinely greenfield" claim is accurate (independently re-grepped `internal/api`/`cmd/api-service` on `origin/main`, found only the inbound `ScopeConnectorIdentityLookup` usage), and `ux-notes.md:27`'s quote is word-for-word exact. No corrections needed.
52	
53	**Tobias Lindqvist — objection:** LT-33 is the first story that gives LT-41's connector rate-limiting a real caller, and nothing in the original draft checked what that control is actually keyed on now that one exists — if it's keyed on caller identity + IP alone (as `backlog.md`'s S8 text reads in isolation), every real onboarding attempt from every end user would share one bucket, unable to distinguish a legitimate retry from enumeration. Checked directly against the actual implementation rather than assumed either way: `identitylimiter.go`'s `identityRateLimiter` already keys on `hashIdentity(callerSub, phone, name)` — the vendor-identity being looked up, not just the caller — so this concern is already correctly resolved by existing code, not a gap. Criterion added as a stated confirmation, per Tobias's own framing that this should be settled explicitly rather than left for a future reader to notice on their own.
54	
55	**Naomi Voss — ruling:** Both reviews came back clean, with Tobias's objection resolving to "already correctly handled, now stated explicitly" rather than new work — a genuinely valuable question that had a good answer, surfaced and confirmed rather than left implicit. This refinement is accepted and ready to hand off.
56	
57	## Update — Marcus's (02) seat, added after initial hand-off
58	
59	02 has a seat on this story's credential handling per its own security surface. Marcus supplied three items, incorporated above: (1) credential holding/rotation — points to `handoff-04-secrets.md` row 10, nothing new; (2) **a genuine correction to this refinement's original draft** — `api-service`'s own connector-scoped token should be cached and reused per-replica until near expiry (standard OAuth2 client-credentials practice), the opposite of what this doc originally specified (which conflated it with the vendor token's correctly-never-cached rule — a different token, a different risk class); (3) the connector-unreachable failure mode — bounded timeout/backoff, generic upward failure, never falls back to stale data, own-token-failure logged as a distinct class from connector-unreachable. All three folded into the acceptance criteria and PO review script above.
60	
61	---
62	Work-By: Naomi Voss (PO, refinement + correction incorporation); Desmond Okafor (claims check); Tobias Lindqvist (rate-limit-keying objection); Marcus Ilori (02, credential-handling spec + the token-caching correction); Renata Cole (03, implementation, pending).

package connector

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"io"
	"net/http"
	"net/url"
	"time"
)

// vendorCallTimeout bounds every outbound vendor call — belt-and-braces
// alongside the caller's own context.WithTimeout, the same
// defense-in-depth pattern LT-40's JWKS fetch client uses (an explicit
// http.Client.Timeout independent of context, since a context deadline
// bug elsewhere must not leave this client waiting forever).
const vendorCallTimeout = 10 * time.Second

// defaultVendorHTTPClient is a bounded-pool client — connector-security.md's
// "bounded connection pool" requirement. Deliberately module-level so
// every HTTPVendorClient constructed without an explicit client shares
// one bounded pool rather than each accidentally getting Go's unbounded
// http.DefaultTransport.
var defaultVendorHTTPClient = &http.Client{
	Timeout: vendorCallTimeout,
	Transport: &http.Transport{
		MaxIdleConns:        20,
		MaxIdleConnsPerHost: 5,
		MaxConnsPerHost:     10,
		IdleConnTimeout:     30 * time.Second,
	},
}

// HTTPVendorClient calls a real (or real-shaped) vendor over HTTPS,
// demonstrating connector-security.md's outbound discipline: TLS only,
// a bounded connection pool, and a fresh, isolated Authorization header
// built inside each call rather than held as client-level state —
// httpClient's connection pooling can reuse a TCP connection across
// calls, but never a header, since baseURL/httpClient are the only
// fields this type holds; the token is a per-call parameter, never
// stored.
//
// No real ABC/XYZ vendor exists for this take-home (LT-41's own
// non-goal) — this implementation assumes a vendor that happens to
// share this project's own JSON request/response shape, a stated
// simplifying assumption for demonstrating the connector's own outbound
// security properties, not a claim about any real vendor's actual API.
type HTTPVendorClient struct {
	baseURL    string
	httpClient *http.Client
}

// NewHTTPVendorClient requires an https:// baseURL — connector-security.md
// §2: "Vendor calls over TLS only ... including in local/dev
// environments." httpClient defaults to defaultVendorHTTPClient (bounded
// pool + timeout) if nil.
func NewHTTPVendorClient(baseURL string, httpClient *http.Client) (*HTTPVendorClient, error) {
	u, err := url.Parse(baseURL)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("connector: parsing vendor base URL: %w", err)
	}
	if u.Scheme != "https" {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("connector: vendor base URL %q must use https, per connector-security.md's TLS-only requirement", baseURL)
	}
	if httpClient == nil {
		httpClient = defaultVendorHTTPClient
	}
	return &HTTPVendorClient{baseURL: baseURL, httpClient: httpClient}, nil
}

type vendorAuthRequest struct {
	Username string `json:"username"`
	Password string `json:"password"`
}

type vendorAuthResponse struct {
	AccessToken string `json:"access_token"`
}

func (c *HTTPVendorClient) Authenticate(ctx context.Context, username, password string) (string, error) {
	reqBody, err := json.Marshal(vendorAuthRequest{Username: username, Password: password})
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("connector: encoding vendor auth request: %w", err)
	}

	// A fresh *http.Request built from scratch on every call — no
	// client-level default header exists to leak across calls, and this
	// request object is never retained past this function returning.
	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.baseURL+"/auth", bytes.NewReader(reqBody))
	if err != nil {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("connector: building vendor auth request: %w", err)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")

	resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		// Never wrap or surface the raw error to our own caller —
		// connector-security.md §4: vendor errors must not distinguish
		// which part of the credential was wrong, and a raw dial/DNS
		// error can itself carry sensitive detail (host, IP). The
		// caller-facing generic failure is this package's caller's job
		// (internal/connector's handlers); this method's contract is
		// simply "success or ErrVendorAuthFailed", nothing richer.
		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
	}
	defer func() { _, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, resp.Body); _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		// Do not read/log the response body — connector-security.md §3:
		// "do not log raw vendor response bodies by default, since a
		// vendor error payload can itself echo back submitted PII or
		// partial credentials."
		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
	}

	var body vendorAuthResponse
	if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20)).Decode(&body); err != nil || body.AccessToken == "" {
		return "", ErrVendorAuthFailed
	}
	return body.AccessToken, nil
}

type vendorIdentityRequest struct {
	Phone string `json:"phone"`
	Name  string `json:"name"`
}

type vendorIdentityResponse struct {
	Name          string `json:"name"`
	Phone         string `json:"phone"`
	StreetAddress string `json:"street_address"`
	Locality      string `json:"locality"`
	Region        string `json:"region"`
	PostalCode    string `json:"postal_code"`
	Country       string `json:"country"`
}

func (c *HTTPVendorClient) FetchIdentity(ctx context.Context, accessToken, phone, name string) (Identity, error) {
	reqBody, err := json.Marshal(vendorIdentityRequest{Phone: phone, Name: name})
	if err != nil {
		return Identity{}, fmt.Errorf("connector: encoding vendor identity request: %w", err)
	}

	req, err := http.NewRequestWithContext(ctx, http.MethodPost, c.baseURL+"/identity", bytes.NewReader(reqBody))
	if err != nil {
		return Identity{}, fmt.Errorf("connector: building vendor identity request: %w", err)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	// The one place accessToken is used — set on this request only,
	// never on a client-level default header, never retained after this
	// function returns.
	req.Header.Set("Authorization", "Bearer "+accessToken)

	resp, err := c.httpClient.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
	}
	defer func() { _, _ = io.Copy(io.Discard, resp.Body); _ = resp.Body.Close() }()

	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusOK {
		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
	}

	var body vendorIdentityResponse
	if err := json.NewDecoder(io.LimitReader(resp.Body, 1<<20)).Decode(&body); err != nil {
		return Identity{}, ErrVendorIdentityNotFound
	}
	// vendorIdentityResponse's fields are identical in name/order/type to
	// Identity (only the json tags differ), so a direct type conversion
	// is both valid and clearer than restating every field (staticcheck
	// S1016).
	return Identity(body), nil
}

// tokenhandler.go implements POST /auth/token — RFC 6749 §4.4's
// client-credentials grant. This is the one endpoint in the API that
// follows OAuth2's own wire format (form-encoded, HTTP Basic client
// auth) rather than this project's usual JSON convention, because it's
// the one endpoint implementing a named external grant type — and, per
// Tobias Lindqvist's objection (LT-51 refinement), the endpoint most
// likely to be pattern-matched against known real-world OAuth2
// timing-oracle bugs by an outside reviewer.
package api

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"log"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/golang-jwt/jwt/v5"
	"github.com/google/uuid"
)

// tokenTTL is this issuer's fixed token lifetime — the middle of
// handoff-03-auth.md's 5-15 minute range. No refresh tokens: a client
// re-authenticates with its own client secret on expiry, per
// api-auth-design.md's standing TTL-only-revocation design (this
// story's own non-goal list; not re-litigated here).
const tokenTTL = 10 * time.Minute

// tokenErrorResponse is RFC 6749 §5.2's error body shape.
type tokenErrorResponse struct {
	Error string `json:"error"`
}

// tokenResponse is RFC 6749 §5.1's success body shape.
type tokenResponse struct {
	AccessToken string `json:"access_token"`
	TokenType   string `json:"token_type"`
	ExpiresIn   int    `json:"expires_in"`
	Scope       string `json:"scope"`
}

func writeTokenError(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, errCode string) {
	if status == http.StatusUnauthorized {
		// RFC 6749 §5.2: invalid_client with a 401 MUST carry
		// WWW-Authenticate identifying the auth scheme this endpoint
		// expects — the same header any HTTP Basic-protected resource
		// returns, not something specific to OAuth2 error semantics.
		w.Header().Set("WWW-Authenticate", `Basic realm="token"`)
	}
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	_ = jsonEncode(w, tokenErrorResponse{Error: errCode})
}

// jsonEncode is a one-line indirection kept trivial and named for
// clarity rather than inlined at each of this file's two call sites.
func jsonEncode(w http.ResponseWriter, v any) error {
	return json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(v)
}

// NewTokenHandler implements POST /auth/token, wiring the collaborators
// named in refinement/LT-51.md's explicit instruction rather than
// reimplementing them: store is the client datastore (this story's own,
// new); limiter is PR #30's already-built GrantLimiter, called exactly
// as its own doc comment specifies — Allow gates before authentication,
// RecordFailure/RecordSuccess run after. The signing key parameter holds
// the loaded RSA private key this story mints tokens with (see
// SigningKey in signingkey.go).
func NewTokenHandler(store ClientStore, limiter GrantLimiter, key *SigningKey, issuer string) http.HandlerFunc {
	return func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		ctx := r.Context()

		// store is nil when the issuer database was never configured
		// (config.Config.IssuerDBDSN empty) — fail every grant closed
		// with server_error rather than panic on a nil ClientStore, the
		// same fail-closed shape NewVerifierRouter uses for a nil
		// dao.Repository. This deliberately runs before ParseForm/
		// BasicAuth so a misconfigured issuer fails identically for
		// every request shape, not just the ones that get far enough to
		// reach store.Get.
		if store == nil {
			writeTokenError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "server_error")
			return
		}

		// RFC 6749 §4.4: form-encoded, not JSON. r.ParseForm reads the
		// body as application/x-www-form-urlencoded regardless of a
		// caller's declared Content-Type, which is deliberately
		// permissive here — this handler's job is accepting the one wire
		// format the spec names, not policing a header a legitimate
		// client-credentials implementation might send inconsistently.
		if err := r.ParseForm(); err != nil {
			writeTokenError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "invalid_request")
			return
		}
		if r.PostForm.Get("grant_type") != "client_credentials" {
			writeTokenError(w, http.StatusBadRequest, "unsupported_grant_type")
			return
		}

		clientID, clientSecret, hasBasicAuth := r.BasicAuth()
		sourceIP := sourceIPFrom(r)

		// GrantLimiter gates BEFORE the hasBasicAuth check, not after —
		// sourceIP is available regardless of whether a caller sent any
		// credentials at all, and a request with no Authorization header
		// is exactly as capable of flooding this endpoint as one with a
		// wrong client_id (Helena Marsh, 02 review of PR #34). clientID
		// is "" on this path; GrantLimiter treats it as an ordinary
		// (empty) key like any other, and the per-source-IP half of the
		// gate is what actually matters here since there's no client_id
		// yet to distinguish attempts by.
		allowed, err := limiter.Allow(ctx, clientID, sourceIP)
		if err != nil {
			writeTokenError(w, http.StatusInternalServerError, "server_error")
			return
		}
		if !allowed {
			// RFC 6749 doesn't name a status for endpoint-level
Shell cwd was reset to /Users/warrenthompson/Source/LoginID/03-engineering-delivery

package app

import (
	"log"
	"net/http"
	"time"

	"loginid-takehome/internal/api"
	"loginid-takehome/internal/config"
	"loginid-takehome/internal/connector"
)

// connectorJWKSCacheTTL matches api-service's own verifier JWKS TTL
// (internal/app/verifier_router.go) — both binaries verify tokens minted
// by the same issuer, so there's no reason for a different refresh
// cadence.
const connectorJWKSCacheTTL = 15 * time.Minute

// connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff/MaxBackoff/AlertThreshold mirror
// NewIssuerRouter's own GrantLimiter constants — same reasoning
// (handoff-03-auth.md names the mechanism, not specific numbers), reused
// here for the vendor-call rate limiter per LT-41's attack-tree leaf 5
// control rather than inventing a second set of magic numbers.
const (
	connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff    = 1 * time.Second
	connectorVendorLimiterMaxBackoff     = 5 * time.Minute
	connectorVendorLimiterAlertThreshold = 5
)

// NewConnectorRouter builds cmd/idp-connector's real router — LT-41's
// /auth and /identity handlers behind the connector's own inbound JWT
// verification (a distinct audience, connector-security.md §5: aud =
// cfg.ConnectorJWTAudience, scope = connector:identity-lookup — NOT
// api-service's audience, so a profile:*-scoped api-service token
// cannot be replayed against this connector and vice versa).
//
// Builds its own JWKSCache from cfg.AuthJWKSURL (same pattern as
// NewVerifierRouter) rather than taking one as a parameter — both
// binaries verify tokens minted by the same issuer, at the same TTL.
//
// vendor is the outbound seam to the third-party IDP — either a real
// (HTTPVendorClient) or stub (StubVendorClient) implementation;
// cmd/idp-connector/main.go decides which based on whether
// IDP_ABC_BASE_URL is configured.
func NewConnectorRouter(cfg config.Config, vendor connector.VendorClient) http.Handler {
	mux := http.NewServeMux()
	mux.HandleFunc("/healthz", healthHandler("idp-connector"))

	auditLog := api.StdoutAuditLogger{}
	keys := api.NewJWKSCache(cfg.AuthJWKSURL, connectorJWKSCacheTTL, nil)
	authMiddleware := api.NewJWTMiddleware(keys, cfg.AuthJWTIssuer, cfg.ConnectorJWTAudience, auditLog)
	protect := func(h http.Handler) http.Handler {
		return api.NewDeadlineMiddleware(authMiddleware(h))
	}

	// One shared limiter instance across both handlers — a caller
	// hammering /auth and a caller hammering /identity are the same
	// abuse signal against the same vendor, keyed by the same
	// (calling-API-client sub, source IP) pair (LT-41 attack-tree
	// leaf 5). Reuses api.GrantLimiter (PR #30/#34) rather than
	// building a new rate-limit type, per this story's own non-goals.
	limiter := api.NewInProcessGrantLimiter(
		connectorVendorLimiterBaseBackoff, connectorVendorLimiterMaxBackoff, connectorVendorLimiterAlertThreshold,
		func(clientID string, consecutiveFailures int) {
			log.Printf("idp-connector: ALERT sustained failed vendor calls from caller sub=%s (%d consecutive)", clientID, consecutiveFailures)
		},
	)

	// The second, per-(caller, attempted identity) dimension leaf 5
	// actually specifies — see internal/connector/identitylimiter.go's
	// own doc comment (Tomasz Wrede's cold review of PR #40).
	identityLimiter := connector.NewIdentityRateLimiter()

	deps := connector.Deps{Vendor: vendor, Limiter: limiter, IdentityLimiter: identityLimiter, AuditLog: auditLog}
	mux.Handle("POST /auth", protect(connector.NewAuthHandler(deps)))
	mux.Handle("POST /identity", protect(connector.NewIdentityHandler(deps)))

	return mux
}

// Package connector implements Q3 — cmd/idp-connector's outbound calls
// to a generic third-party identity provider ("ABC"/"XYZ" in the
// assignment's own generic framing), per
// 02-ai-security-architecture/connector-security.md and
// refinement/LT-41.md. No real vendor integration exists or is asked
// for (LT-41's own non-goals) — VendorClient is the seam a real vendor
// adapter would implement; this package ships a stub (stubvendorclient.go)
// good enough to demonstrate the connector's own contract and security
// properties end to end, and an HTTP-based implementation
// (httpvendorclient.go) that demonstrates the outbound security
// discipline connector-security.md requires, callable against any vendor
// that happens to share this project's own JSON shape (a stated
// simplifying assumption, not a claim about any real vendor's actual
// API).
package connector

import "context"

// Identity is the PII a vendor's /identity endpoint returns, matching
// the assignment's own field list verbatim — name, phone, and address
// (street_address, locality, region, postal_code, country).
type Identity struct {
	Name          string
	Phone         string
	StreetAddress string
	Locality      string
	Region        string
	PostalCode    string
	Country       string
}

// VendorClient is this connector's seam to a third-party IDP. Both
// methods take the vendor bearer token as an explicit parameter, never a
// client-level field — connector-security.md §1's fetch-use-zeroize
// boundary requires the token's scope to run from the point it's read
// off the request to the end of that single call, which a client-level
// field would silently violate by keeping it alive for the client's own
// lifetime instead.
type VendorClient interface {
	// Authenticate exchanges an end-user's vendor username/password for
	// a vendor access token, per the assignment's own POST /auth
	// contract. Returns a generic error on any failure — the specific
	// reason (unknown username vs. wrong password vs. vendor
	// unreachable) is for this package's own internal handling only,
	// never surfaced to our caller (connector-security.md §4:
	// "must not leak which part of the credential was wrong").
	Authenticate(ctx context.Context, username, password string) (accessToken string, err error)

	// FetchIdentity looks up PII by phone/name using an already-obtained
	// vendor accessToken, per the assignment's own POST /identity
	// contract. accessToken is used for this one call only and is never
	// retained by the implementation past this call returning.
	FetchIdentity(ctx context.Context, accessToken, phone, name string) (Identity, error)
}

package connector

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"io"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"loginid-takehome/internal/api"
)

// identityFailureFloor is /identity's uniform response-time floor across
// its three failure branches (no-token, bad-token, vendor-rejected) —
// Marcus Ilori's (02) ruling on Tobias Lindqvist's timing-oracle
// objection (refinement/LT-41.md), recorded in full in
// connector-security.md §4. ~100ms, well under any context.WithTimeout
// budget elsewhere in the chain (vendorCallTimeout is 10s) so it never
// competes with those deadlines. This narrows, but does not fully close,
// the gap between a local-only failure (microseconds) and a
// vendor-round-trip failure (tens-hundreds of ms) — full closure is an
// accepted non-goal per that ruling.
const identityFailureFloor = 100 * time.Millisecond

// identityErrorBody is the one JSON shape every /identity failure
// returns, regardless of which of the three branches produced it —
// content-identical by construction (a single writeIdentityFailure call
// site below), not by three call sites happening to independently agree.
type identityErrorBody struct {
	Error string `json:"error"`
}

// vendorLimiter is the interface these handlers depend on — satisfied
// directly by api.GrantLimiter (PR #30/#34's already-built component,
// reused here rather than rebuilt per LT-41's own non-goals: "this
// story's own in-process rate limiting is sufficient, matching the
// pattern already used for RateLimiter/TouchCounter/GrantLimiter").
// Declared locally so this package's own tests can supply a minimal fake
// satisfying only the calls actually made here, per
// decisions/test-double-strategy.md.
type vendorLimiter interface {
	Allow(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string) (bool, error)
	RecordFailure(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
	RecordSuccess(ctx context.Context, clientID, sourceIP string)
}

// Deps bundles NewAuthHandler/NewIdentityHandler's collaborators.
//
// IdentityLimiter is the second rate-limit dimension attack-tree leaf 5
// actually specifies ("per identity attempted, not just per client") —
// Limiter alone (keyed by calling-API-client sub + source IP) means one
// success anywhere resets that caller's whole consecutive-failure count,
// letting a compromised caller cycle through many stolen identities
// indefinitely (Tomasz Wrede's cold review, PR #40). May be nil in tests
// that don't exercise this dimension; NewConnectorRouter always
// constructs a real one.
type Deps struct {
	Vendor          VendorClient
	Limiter         vendorLimiter
	IdentityLimiter *identityRateLimiter
	AuditLog        api.AuditLogger
}

// identityGateAllow/RecordFailure/RecordSuccess are deps.IdentityLimiter's
// call sites, tolerant of a nil IdentityLimiter (tests that only care
// about the caller-level Limiter dimension can omit it — the identity
// dimension then simply isn't enforced, never a panic). identityParts is
// variadic so a multi-field identity (phone AND name) can be hashed as
// separate components rather than pre-joined with a printable separator
// — see identitylimiter.go's hashIdentity doc comment.
func identityGateAllow(ctx context.Context, l *identityRateLimiter, callerSub string, identityParts ...string) (bool, error) {
	if l == nil {
		return true, nil
	}
	return l.allow(ctx, callerSub, identityParts...)
}

func identityGateRecordFailure(ctx context.Context, l *identityRateLimiter, callerSub string, identityParts ...string) {
	if l == nil {
		return
	}
	l.recordFailure(ctx, callerSub, identityParts...)
}

func identityGateRecordSuccess(ctx context.Context, l *identityRateLimiter, callerSub string, identityParts ...string) {
	if l == nil {
		return
	}
	l.recordSuccess(ctx, callerSub, identityParts...)
}

func sourceIPFrom(r *http.Request) string {
	host, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(r.RemoteAddr)
	if err != nil {
		return r.RemoteAddr
	}
	return host
}

func writeJSON(w http.ResponseWriter, status int, v any) {
	w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	w.WriteHeader(status)
	_ = json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(v)
}

// writeIdentityFailure is the single call site for every /identity
// failure response — the one place that guarantees byte-identical
// content across the no-token, bad-token, and vendor-rejected branches
// (connector-security.md §1/F3), never three call sites independently
// trying to agree.
func writeIdentityFailure(w http.ResponseWriter) {
	writeJSON(w, http.StatusUnauthorized, identityErrorBody{Error: "identity_lookup_failed"})
}

// waitForFloor sleeps out the remainder of identityFailureFloor since
// start, if any — applied uniformly across /identity's three failure
// branches so none of them is distinguishable from the others by
// response latency alone (partial mitigation per connector-security.md
